1  INTRODUCTION AND SCOPE

Purpose:

The purpose of this manual is to provide the procedures required to conduct safe
and efficient drilling operations on the Semi Submersible Iran Amir Kabir.

Scope:

This manual discusses the various aspects of drilling operations, including the
methods and procedures available, and the corresponding equipment involved.
The manual will also include the purpose, scope, safety precautions/procedures
and the individual responsibilities for each procedure. The use and efficiency of
any method, procedure or operation, however, depends on the experience and
performance of the personnel involved and the condition of the equipment to be
used.  All operations require correct analysis, proper planning, sound judgment,
trained personnel and common sense.
These procedures are provided as a guide only, because the Operator may use
different methods and programs for drilling an offshore well. Aban, as the drilling
contractor, adapts  its  drilling operations accordingly but  in compliance with
accepted safe oilfield practices and the applicable regulations.


Establishing the well:

The oil company that has contracted the vessel for the drilling operation (the
Operator) must survey the location before the arrival of the vessel on site and will
place the positioning equipment on the rig. The exact location with detail of well
coordinates will be communicated to the Master and OIM on duty. On most
occasions the Operator  will arrange  for the location survey and positioning
equipment through a well experienced  third party who  will also witness the
positioning of the rig immediately prior to spud.

4  DRILLING PROCEDURES


4.1 PHASE DEPENDENT PROCEDURES


4.1.1 Spudding In

Commencement of drilling the well, the Spudding in, must be in accordance with
the Operator’s plan and a Pre-Spud Meeting should take place before starting.
All the parties involved should participate in this meeting to review, agree and
adhere to the policies and procedures for both routine and non-routine
operations. Where parties have differences in policy and procedures, mutually
acceptable operations shall be agreed to, and any perceived area of well control
procedural conflict shall be resolved before commencing the operation.

4.1.2  Drilling 26”/36” Hole Section

Purpose:

In areas where the seabed is hard and heavily bouldered a 36” hole may be
drilled and the 30” casing cemented in place. The purpose of this procedure is to
ensure a smooth and safe operation while drilling the 26”/36” hole section.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the activities that are to be carried out prior to
and during the drilling of the 26”/36” hole section.

Responsible Persons:

Senior Toolpusher / Tourpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all the activities of this process.
   •  Ensure  that the  drilling and associated operations are carried out  in
      accordance with the defined operations programme and are conducted in
     a safe and efficient manner.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of the Drilling and associated operations.
   •  Ensure that the unit has sufficient resources and supplies to perform
        drilling and associated activities as per contractual requirements.
   •  Confirm any calculations made are correct.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.
   •  Measure and tally the next casing string.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure that accurate tallies and depth control is maintained.
   •  Inform the on  tour Toolpusher and Operator  Drilling Supervisor   if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.

Assistant Driller:

   •  Maintain the pipe  tally,  drilling  tally and BHA (Bottom Hole Assembly)
      records accurately and up to date.
   •  Supervise and assist with drill floor, pump/pit room, shaker house, mixing
     and fluid conditioning operations.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                 7


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 8 از 286 ]

                         Issue Date

   •  Pre-plan and make ready the correct tools, equipment and necessary
     manpower for forthcoming operations.
   •  Ensure that the Driller’s instructions to crewmembers are carried out.
   •  Bring to the attention of the Driller anything that may affect the safe and
     smooth running of the operation.
   •  Assist third party personnel involved in drilling operations.
   •  Check, measure, document and inspect all tubulars and drilling tools to be
      run in the hole.
   •  Assist with deck operations when required.

Deck Crew:

   •  Transfer of equipment to and from the drill floor in the correct sequence as
       required.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparations:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3. Pre-Job Meeting held with all the parties involved.
    4.  Lifting gear inspected.
    5. PPE worn at all times.
    6. Weather conditions in the working range of the rig.
    7. The drilling programme available and instructions given in writing to the
         driller.
    8. The mud  characteristics and mud volume  ready as  per  the mud
      programme.
    9.  All BHA components to be measured with  all dimensions recorded on
      drawings and ready for picking up. This includes the drill bit and nozzles.
   10. A hole cover will be in place when ongoing activities associated with the
      removal and replacement of rotary table components are suspended for
      even the shortest period of time.
   11. Measure the casing to determine the depth of the 36" hole to be drilled,
        this may or may not include a rathole as per the drilling programme.
   12. Inspect and service the casing running tools.
   13. Make up the cementing/circulating head with a side outlet sub to a joint of
        drifted landing  string and rack  this  in the  derrick. The OIM, Senior
      Toolpusher or Tourpusher, Operator  drilling supervisor and cementer
      should be in attendance.
   14. Mix  the  initial volume  of mud as  required as per  the  drilling mud
      programme.
   15. Pick up and rack at least enough drill pipe to complete the next section.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                 8


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 9 از 286 ]

                         Issue Date

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   16. Make up the 30” casing and funnel guide base, and skid it to the Port-side
       of the moonpool ensuring it is well secured.
   17. Install slope indicators on the funnel guide base.
   18. Master to confirm rig position before spudding the well.

Operation:

    1. A Rig Specific Procedure for handling the Master Bushings during riserless
       conditions, running surface casing, BOP and riser operations will be in
      place and followed (Procedure 4.2.8.2 – Pulling the Master Bushings
      while Riserless).
    2. A hole cover will be in place when ongoing activities associated with the
      removal and replacement of rotary table components are suspended for
      even the shortest period of time.
    3.  If shallow gas is suspected then a pilot hole (12 ¼”) will be drilled to the
      20” casing setting depth adjacent to the well location prior to moving onto
       location and spudding the well.
    4. Shallow gas procedures will be implemented for this section as detailed in
      the well control manual.
    5. Make up and run the 26” bit and 36” BHA as per the drilling programme
       (Paint the bit and hole opener white).
    6. A shock sub may be required  if hard formations  / boulders may be
      encountered.
    7. Run in to just above the sea bed. Master to verify the ships location. Space
      out to tag the seabed near the start of a stand. Open the DSC (Drill String
      Compensator).
    8. Wait for slack water at high or low tide and with the ROV observing run in
       with the spud assembly and without  circulation tag the seabed with
      minimal weight.
    9. At this point an accurate rotary table to seabed measurement must be
       taken, adjusted for tide and recorded in the IADC drilling report.
   10. Without  circulation  or  rotation apply  weight, observe and record the
     amount of free penetration.
   11. Drill  the  26”/36”  hole  with  reduced  parameters as  per  the  drilling
      programme. Once the hole opener is well below the mud line increase the
      parameters as per the programme.
   12. A low hole angle should be maintained throughout the section. To help
      achieve this, a low WOB in conjunction with high RPM should be utilised.
   13. Drill to casing depth with seawater allowing for the height of the hole
      opener above the bit.
   14. Pump viscous sweeps as required and prior to connections.  Take any
       directional surveys as per the drilling program.
   15. If hole cleaning becomes a problem, then increase the frequency of the
     sweeps or the volume of the sweeps, repeat as required.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                 9


────────────────────────────────────────────

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   16. At casing depth take a directional survey and  if within the parameters
     sweep the hole with a viscous pill and then displace the hole to viscous
     mud. Pull out to just below the mud line.
   17. If the survey  is outside the parameters inform the OIM and Operator
        Drilling Supervisor.
   18. Wait 1 hour. Run in to bottom and note the  fill. If bridges or excessive  fill
      are noticed, then repeat the hole displacement and wiper trips until the
      hole is in an acceptable condition to run the casing.
   19. It may be necessary to displace the hole with weighted viscous mud to
      keep it open.
   20. When the hole is in a satisfactory condition displace  it with viscous mud
     and pull out to run the casing.
   21. If clean pull out of the hole ensuring that the ROV takes a distance and
      bearing to the hole before the BHA is pulled out of the hole.
   22. Rack back the BHA; do not break off the bit.
   23. Rig up to run casing if not already made up and hung off in the moonpool.
   24. If the ROV has to be recovered to deck for any reason, a sonar deflector
      should be left on the seabed to enable the hole to be located easily when
      the ROV is returned into service.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clean up the drill floor.
    2. Change over to casing running equipment.
    3. Return all lifting gear and safety equipment to designated storage area.
    4. Check  all  lifting gear and slings.  If damaged they should be cut and
      discarded.
    5. Close out Work Permits on the completion of the task.
    6. Return all radios to the Control room.
    7. Skid the 30” casing under the rotary.
    8. Ensure the slope indicators are installed on the funnel guide base.

4.1.3  Running and Cementing 30” Conductor Pipe

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
running and cementing the 30” casing.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the running and cementing of the 30” casing.

Responsible Persons:

Senior Toolpusher / Tourpusher

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  To ensure that the operations are carried out in accordance with the
      defined operations programme and are conducted in a safe and efficient
      manner.
   •  Supervise operations in the moon pool.
   •  Confirm casing  tallies, running order and cementing programme are
       correct.
   •  Consult with the OIM and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of operations.
   •  Keep the Master informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Assist as required with moonpool activities.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure tallies and depth measurements are accurate.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.



Crane Operator / Deck Crew:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                11


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 12 از 286 ]

                         Issue Date

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   •  Supervise and conduct all deck operations associated with this process.
   •  Support all rig floor and moon pool activities as required.
   •  Bring to the attention of the on tour Toolpusher any occurrence that may
       affect the safe and smooth efficient running of operations.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. A Permit to Work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3. A Pre-Job meeting to be held with all the parties involved. The procedure
     and JSA to be discussed.
    4.  Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc.).
    5. PPE to be worn all the times (helmets with chin straps).
    6.  All non essential personnel to be kept clear of the Drill floor & Casing Bay.
    7. Ensure weather conditions are within the working range of the rig.
    8. Ensure the BOP cranes are stowed Stb and Portside in the moonpool.

Preparation Prior to Running Casing:

    1. A casing tally to be ready with joint numbers, running order etc made
       available to the driller and deck crew.
    2. Shoe and housing joint painted as per client requirement.
    3.  Drill ahead running tool ready and racked in the derrick or on the pipe
      deck.
    4. Make up the wellhead housing running tool with a joint of drifted landing
       string above the tool and a pup joint below of sufficient length, so that the
     pup joint pin end will extend out of the well head housing joint by +/- 1.5m.
      Lay this assembly out onto the deck. Personnel involved OIM, Senior
      Toolpusher or Tourpusher, Operator  Drilling Supervisor and Wellhead
      Service Engineer all to be present on drill floor.
    5. Pick up the wellhead housing, pick up the running tool assembly and make
            it  up   to   the   wellhead   housing   following   the   manufacturers'
      recommendations.
    6. Paint a vertical white line from 3ft above the tool joint above the running
        tool, through the running tool onto the housing and down onto the casing
      below by 3ft. This is to visually check that the running tool does not back
      out when the housing is later made up to the casing.
    7. Lay out the housing and running tool assembly.
    8. Under no circumstances should the housing be racked in the derrick as
     movement of the rig or accidental monkey board release could cause
      permanent bending of the stand and possible injury to personnel.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                12


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 13 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011

                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    9. The funnel guide base ready and secured in the moon pool with the slope
       indicator installed and zeroed.
   10. Safety goggles required for the locking tabs – either using hammer or Hilti
      gun. A PTW (Permit to Work) is required for this operation.
   11. Check the condition of rig floor air winch wires and swivels. Ensure all
      shackles are 4 part with pin retainers installed.
   12. Check that back up conductor and casing joints are available.
   13. Prepare the  jetting  /  drilling bottom hole assembly and  cut  the 30”
      conductor shoe to ensure the 26” drilling bit (painted white) will be 0.3m
      below the shoe.
   14. Mix the required quantities of gel mud as per the mud program.
   15. Install a 50 ton 10m sling on each of the bails with a 55ton shackle.
   16. Install a set of the 30” elevators on the slings ensuring the bail retainers
      are installed properly.
   17. If running on pad eyes install two 55ton shackles on the bottom of each
        sling.
   18. Verify the 30” housing will pass through the rotary.

Running 30” Conductor on Padeyes:

    1. Place the first joint of 30” CP (Conductor Pipe) conductor on the catwalk.
    2. Rig for lifting with the crane and a 5 ton sling 1m from the bottom and the
          drill floor air winch and a 5 ton sling 2m from the top. (Two  drill floor
      winches and slings can be used if required).
    3. Attach the catwalk air winch and a sling to the bottom of the conductor to
       stabilize it.
    4. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
      catwalk winch driver and deck foreman hoist the conductor to the drill floor
      keeping it slightly below horizontal to prevent it swinging into the drill floor
     when it reaches the top of the V door ramp.
    5. Slowly bring the conductor into the  drill floor, land  it on the deck and
      prepare it as per the casing service hand instruction. Remove the protector
     and prepare the joint as per the casing service hand instructions. Ensure o
       rings are fitted to the pin and box as required.
    6.  Prior to lifting the conductor with the blocks, remove the master bushings.
    7. Shackle each sling on the top drive to one of the pad eyes on the
      conductor with and take some weight.
    8. Remove the drill floor winch and sling.
    9. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, driller, catwalk
      winch  driver and deck foreman,  hoist  the  conductor  simultaneously
       slacking off on the catwalk winch and crane, keeping the bottom of the
      pipe clear of the deck.
   10. When near vertical, extend the tailing arm, slack off until the joint rests
       against the tailing arm and remove the crane and catwalk airwinch.
   11. Once secure in the rotary, retract the tailing arm and lower the catwalk
       airwinch to the main deck using the V door winch.

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   12. Run the joint in the hole monitoring the progress on the moonpool CCTV
      (Closed Circuit Television).
   13. Land the pad eyes on the rotary and unshackle the slings.
   14. Prepare the next joint of conductor and pick it up as per steps 1-11.
   15. When the bottom of the joint is at the drill floor take the weight with the
        tailing arm and remove the crane, catwalk air winch and protector. Prepare
      the pin as per the casing service hand instructions.
   16. Make up the joint as per the casing service hand instruction instructions.
   17. If necessary turn rotary table/pipe handler until the anti-rotation slot and pin
      are aligned, when aligned lower the blocks until the joint lands out, check
      the snap ring has energized, confirm with a test lift of the space conductor.
    A Hilti gun may be used on some joints. Tongs may also be used to make
     up to the required torque.
   18. Remove the catwalk air winch and sling.
   19. Take the weight of both joints and cut the pad eyes off as close as possible
       to the conductor but ensuring the conductor is not damaged. Use the
        tailing arm to steady the conductor while the pad eyes are cut.
   20. Lower the conductor monitoring progress on the moon pool CCTV to stab
      the 1st joint into the guide base. The AD or Toolpusher to guide the driller
      from the cellar deck.
   21. Run the remaining 30’’ CP (as per the tally).
   22. Pick up the 30” Housing and make it up to the last 30” CP and set it in the
       rotary.
   23. Run the combined jetting  / drilling BHA inside the 30”conductor string.
     Take the weight of the jetting/ drilling BHA and connect the drill ahead tool
       to the 30’’ housing. Confirm the bit position is 0.3m below the shoe with the
     ROV.
   24. Take the weight of the string and cut the pad eyes off as close as possible
       to the housing but ensuring the conductor is not damaged. Use the tailing
     arm to steady the conductor while the pad eyes are cut.
   25. Lower the 30” CP and land in the guide base. Install locking bolts as
       required.
   26. Pick up the string and visually check the guide base slope indicator reads
       zero.
   27. Retract the support beams and RIH the conductor string taking the weight
       of the assembly once submerged. Subtract the weight of the jetting/ drilling
    BHA for the weight of the conductor string and guide base.

Running the 30” Conductor on Elevators:

    1. Place the first joint of 30” CP (Conductor Pipe) on the catwalk.
    2. Rig for lifting with the crane and a 5 ton sling 1m from the bottom and the
          drill floor air winch and a 5 ton sling 2m from the top. (Two  drill floor
      winches and slings can be used if required).
    3. Attach the catwalk air winch and a sling to the bottom of the conductor to
       stabilize it.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                14

4. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
   catwalk winch driver and deck foreman hoist the 30”CP to the drill floor
   keeping it slightly below horizontal to prevent it swinging into the drill floor
   when it reaches the top of the V door ramp.
5. Slowly bring the 30”CP into the drill floor and land it on the deck. Remove
   the protector and prepare the box as per the Operator Drilling Supervisor
    instructions.
6.  Prior  to  lifting  the conductor  into  the  elevators remove  the master
   bushings.
7. Rotate the side door elevators to face the V door and open them.
8. Pick up the 30”CP and land it firmly in the elevators to swing the elevators
    vertical and close them.
9. Confirm the elevators are properly latched and install the retaining pin (if
    fitted).
10. Remove the drill floor winch and sling.
11. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, driller catwalk
   winch driver and deck foreman hoist the 30”CP simultaneously slacking off
   on the catwalk winch and crane keeping the bottom of the pipe clear of the
   deck.
12. When near vertical, extend the tailing arm, slack off until the tailing arms
   rests against the trailing arm. Remove the crane and catwalk airwinch.
13. Once secure in the rotary, retract the tailing arm and lower the catwalk
   airwinch to the main deck using the V door winch.
14. Run the joint in the hole monitoring the progress on the moon pool CCTV.
15. Land the elevators on the rotary and remove the bail retainers and slings.
16. Prepare the second set of elevators, ensuring they correctly orientated,
   and install the slings and bail retainers.
17. Prepare the next joint of 30”CP and pick it up as per steps 1-12.
18. When the bottom of the joint is at the drill floor take the weight with the
    tailing arm and remove the crane, catwalk air winch and protector. Prepare
   the pin as per the Operator’s Drilling Supervisor instructions.
19. Orientate the joint as required, stab  it onto the joint in the rotary and
   remove the tailing arm.
20. Make up the joint as per the Operator Drilling Supervisor instructions.
21. Lower the 30”CP monitoring progress on the moon pool CCTV to stab the
   1st joint into the guide base. The AD or Toolpusher to guide the driller from
   the cellar deck.
22. Run the remaining 30”CP as per the tally.
23. Pick up the 30” Housing, make  it up to the last 30”CP and set  it in the
    rotary.
24. Run the combined jetting / drilling BHA inside the conductor string. Take
   the weight of the jetting/ drilling BHA and connect the drill ahead tool to the
    30’’ housing and confirm the bit position with ROV (0.3m below the shoe).
25. Run in and land in the funnel guide base in the moonpool. Secure the
   housing to the guide base as per the wellhead service hand instruction.

   26. Pick up the string and visually check the guide base slope indicator reads
       zero.

      weight of the assembly once submerged. Subtract the weight of the jetting/
        drilling BHA from this for the weight of the conductor string and guide base.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Tidy and clear the rig floor of casing handling equipment.
    2. Return all lifting gear and safety equipment to designated storage area.
    3. Check  all  lifting gear and slings.  If damaged they should be cut and

    4. Prepare for the next operation.
    5. Close out Work Permits on the completion of the task.
    6. Return all radios to the Control room.
    7. Deck crew to clear away all transit slings and prepare to back load excess
      conductor.
    8. Service the mud pump fluid ends and change piston and liner sizes as
       required
    9. Clean high and low pressure mud pump strainers.
   10. Review the procedure and JSA.

4.1.4 Run and Jet the 30” Conductor Pipe

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
running and jetting the 30” conductor pipe.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the running and jetting of the 30” conductor pipe.

Responsible Persons:

Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  To ensure that the operations are carried out in accordance with the
      defined operations programme and are conducted in a safe and efficient
      manner.
   •  Supervise operations in the moon pool.
   •  Confirm casing  tallies, running order and cementing programme are
       correct.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of operations.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Assist as required with moonpool activities.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure tallies and depth measurements are accurate.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.



Crane Operator / Deck Crew:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                17


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 18 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.

                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations

                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   •  Supervise and conduct all deck operations associated with this process.
   •  Support all rig floor and moon pool activities as required.
   •  Bring to the attention of the on tour Toolpusher any occurrence that may
       affect the safe and smooth efficient running of operations.

Responsible Persons:

Procedure:

Safety Precautions:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. A Permit to Work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3. A Pre-Job meeting to be held with all the parties involved. The procedure
     and JSA to be discussed.
    4.  Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc.).
    5. PPE to be worn all the times (helmets with chin straps).
    6.  All non essential personnel to be kept clear of the Drill floor & Casing Bay.
    7. Ensure weather conditions are within the working range of the rig.
    8. Ensure the BOP cranes are stowed fore and aft in the moonpool.

Preparation:

    1. A casing tally to be ready with joint numbers, running order etc. made
       available to the driller and deck crew.
    2. Shoe and housing joint painted as per client requirement.
    3.  Drill ahead running tool ready and racked in the derrick or on the pipe
      deck.
    4.  Drift the landing string and any pup joints used.
    5. Make up a joint of drifted landing string to the top of the 18 ¾” wellhead
      housing, running tool and make up a pup  joint to the bottom of  it of
        sufficient length to protrude +/-1.5m out of the bottom of the wellhead
      housing joint. Lay this assembly out onto the deck.
    6. Pick up the 18 ¾” wellhead housing, pick up the running tool assembly and
     make  it up to the wellhead housing as per the wellhead service hand
       instructions.
    7. Paint a vertical white line from 1m above the tool joint above the running
        tool, through the running tool onto the housing and down onto the casing
      below by 1m. This is to visually check that the running tool does not back
      out when the housing is later made up to the casing.
    8. Lay out the 18 ¾” wellhead housing and running tool assembly.




                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                18

    9. Under no circumstances should the housing be racked in the derrick as
     movement of the rig or accidental monkey board release could cause
      permanent bending of the stand and possible injury to personnel.
   10. The funnel guide base ready and secured in the moon pool with the slope
       indicators installed and zeroed.
   11. Safety goggles required for the locking tabs – either using hammer or Hilti
      gun. A PTW (Permit to Work) is required for this operation.
   12. Check the condition of rig floor air winch wires and swivels. Ensure all
      shackles are 4-part with pin retainers installed.
   13. Check that back up conductor and casing joints are available.
   14. Prepare the  jetting  /  drilling bottom hole assembly and  cut the 30”
      conductor shoe to ensure the 26” drilling bit (painted white) will be 0.3m
      below the shoe.
   15. Mix the required quantities of gel mud as per the mud program.
   16. Install a 50 ton 10m sling on each of the bails with a 55ton shackle.
   17. Install a set of the 30” elevators on the slings ensuring the bail retainers
      are installed properly.
   18. If running on pad eyes install two 55ton shackles on the bottom of each
        sling.
   19. Verify the 30” housing will pass through the rotary.

Running 30’’ Conductor on Padeyes:

    1. Place the first joint of 30” CP (Conductor Pipe) on the catwalk.
    2. Rig for lifting with the crane and a 5 ton sling 1m from the bottom and the
          drill floor air winch and a 5 ton sling 2m from the top. (Two  drill floor
      winches and slings can be used if required).
    3. Attach the catwalk air winch and a sling to the bottom of the conductor to
       stabilize it.
    4. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
      catwalk winch driver and deck foreman, hoist the conductor to the drill floor
      keeping it slightly below horizontal to prevent it swinging into the drill floor
     when it reaches the top of the V door ramp.
    5. Slowly bring the conductor into the  drill floor, land  it on the deck and
      prepare it the casing service hand instructions (If used, ensure o rings are
         fitted).
    6.  Prior to lifting the conductor with the blocks remove the master bushings.
    7. Shackle each sling on the top drive to one of the pad eyes on the
      conductor and take some weight.
    8. Remove the drill floor air winch and sling.
    9. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, driller, catwalk
      winch  driver and deck foreman,  hoist  the  conductor  simultaneously
       slacking off on the catwalk winch and crane keeping the bottom of the pipe
       clear of the deck.
   10. When near vertical, extend the tailing arm, slack off until the tailing arms
       rests against the trailing arm. Remove the crane and catwalk airwinch.

   11. Once secure in the rotary, retract the tailing arm and lower the catwalk

   15. When the bottom of the joint is at the drill floor take the weight with the
      catwalk air winch and remove the protector. Prepare the pin as per the

    2. Rig for lifting with the crane and a 5 ton sling 1m from the bottom and the
          drill floor air winch and a 5 ton sling 2m from the top. (Two  drill floor

3. Attach the catwalk air winch and a sling to the bottom of the conductor to
    stabilize it.

   catwalk winch driver and deck foreman, hoist the 30” CP to the drill floor
   keeping it slightly below horizontal to prevent it swinging into the drill floor
   when it reaches the top of the V door ramp.

   the  protector and prepare the box as per the casing  service hand
    instructions.

   bushings.

8. Pick up the 30” CP and land it firmly in the elevators to swing the elevators
    vertical and close them.

    fitted).

  ROV that the bit position is 0.3m below the shoe.

[ صفحه 22 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011

                               0

   25. Take the weight of the CP and jetting assembly, remove the elevators, run
        in and set the CP in the funnel guide base in the moonpool. Secure the
      housing to the guide base as per the wellhead service hand instruction.
   26. Pick up the string and visually check the guide base slope indicators read
       zero.
   27. Retract the support beams and run in with the conductor string taking the
      weight of the assembly once submerged. Subtract the weight of the jetting/
        drilling BHA from this for the weight of the conductor string and guide base.

Jet the 30” conductor:

    1. Run the conductor on the landing string until just above the seabed with
      the ROV observing. Space out to allow a full stand for jetting the CP prior
       to making a connection. DP will verify the ships location.
    2. Open the CMC (Crown Mounted Compensator) and wait for slack water at
      high or low tide  if required and with the ROV observing, run in with the
      spud assembly, without circulation tag the seabed with minimal weight.
    3. At this point an accurate rotary table to seabed measurement must be
       taken, adjusted for tide and recorded in the IADC drilling report.
    4. Without  circulation  or  rotation apply  weight, observe and record the
     amount of free penetration.
    5. Commence jetting the 30” CP with seawater at low pump strokes initially
         (first 5-10m) and minimal weight with no rotation.
    6. Use the minimum weight  to keep the  inclination as near  vertical as
       possible. Observe the slope indicators on the guide base with the ROV
      throughout the jetting operation.
    7. Gradually increase to maximum pump strokes using seawater.
    8. Pump high viscosity slugs when required to flush out the 30” casing.
    9. Observe the returns with the ROV to confirm circulation is coming from
       inside the 30” CP.
   10. Reduce the pump rate over the last 3-4 m of penetration to prevent
      excessive hole washout.
   11. Jet the 30” CP until the top of 30” housing is at the correct stick up as per
      the drilling programme (usually +/- 2m).
   12. At setting depth stop circulation and wait for 1 or 2 hours for the 30” CP to
      soak.
   13. Slack  off the weight  of the conductor and observe  for signs  of the
      conductor sinking either by the marks on the conductor or an increase in
       weight. Check the slope indicators read less than 1.5deg.
   14. If the 30" conductor sinks, take the weight again and allow it to soak for a
       further period of time before trying again. Repeat as required.
   15. If previous operations in the same area of operation has shown that the
      30” conductor tends to sink, three precautions could be taken:
          •  Use a mud mat.
          •  Run extra 30” conductor to increase the skin effect.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                22

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

          •  Space out to place the bit further inside the shoe to avoid washout.
   16. Where the bottom is hard sand a down-hole motor can be run.
   17. Once the 30” conductor is stable and at less than 1.5deg the string can be
      released from the drill-ahead tool by turning to the left.
   18. The 26” hole is drilled as per procedure 4.1.5 - Drill 26” Hole to the setting
      depth of the 20” casing.
   19. When pulling out of the hole recover the  drill ahead tool from the 30”
      housing as per procedure 4.1.5 - Drill 26” Hole.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Tidy and clear the rig floor of casing handling equipment.
    2. Check  all  lifting gear and slings.  If damaged they should be cut and
      discarded.
    3. Return all lifting gear and safety equipment to designated storage area.
    4. Prepare for the next operation.
    5. Close out Work Permits on the completion of the task.
    6. Return all radios to the Control room.
    7. Deck crew to clear away all transit slings and prepare to back load excess
      conductor.
    8. Service the mud pump fluid ends and change piston and liner sizes as
       required
    9. Clean high and low pressure mud pump strainers.
   10. Review the procedure and JSA.

4.1.5   Drilling 26” Hole

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure a smooth and safe operation while
drilling a 26” hole section.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the activities that are to be carried out prior to
and during the drilling of 26” hole section.

Responsible Persons:

Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  Ensure  that the  drilling and associated operations are carried out  in
      accordance with the defined operations programme and are conducted in
     a safe and efficient manner.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of the Drilling and associated operations.
   •  Ensure that the unit has sufficient resources and supplies to perform
        drilling and associated activities as per contractual requirements.
   •  Confirm the calculations made by the driller.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.
   •  Measure and tally the next casing string.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this  process.
   •  Ensure that accurate tallies and depth control is maintained.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.

Assistant Driller:

   •  Maintain pipe tally, drilling tally and BHA records accurately and up to date.
   •  Supervise and assist with drill floor, pump/pit room, shaker house, mixing
     and fluid conditioning operations.
   •  Pre-plan and make ready the correct tools, equipment and necessary
     manpower for forthcoming operations.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                24


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 25 از 286 ]

   •  Ensure that the Driller’s instructions to crewmembers are carried out.
   •  Bring to the attention of the Driller anything that may affect the safe and
     smooth running of the operation.
   •  Assist third party personnel involved in drilling operations.
   •  Check, measure document and inspect tubulars and drilling tools to be run
        in the hole.
   •  Assist with deck operations when required.

Deck Crew:

   •  Transfer of equipment to and from the drill floor in the correct sequence as
       required.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3.  Lifting gear inspected.
    4. PPE worn at all times.
    5. Weather conditions in the working range of the rig.
    6. The drilling programme available and instructions given in writing to the
         driller.
    7. The mud characteristics and mud volume ready as per programme.
    8.  All BHA (Bottom Hole Assembly) components to be measured with  all
      dimensions recorded on drawings and ready for picking up. This includes
      the drill bit and nozzles.
    9. A hole cover will be in place when ongoing activities associated with the
      removal and replacement of rotary table components are suspended for
      even the shortest period of time.
   10. Measure the casing to determine the depth of the 26" hole to be drilled,
       allowing for the rat hole required below the casing shoe (typically +/- 6m).
   11. Inspect and service the casing running tools.
   12. Make up the cementing/circulating head with a side outlet sub to a joint of
        drifted landing string and rack this in the derrick. Rig Superintendent,
       senior toolpusher, toolpusher, all to be present on the drill floor.
   13. Mix the initial volume of mud as per the drilling mud programme.
   14. Pick up and rack at least enough drill pipe to complete the next section.

Operation Overview:

    1. Make up and run 26” BHA and 30” casing as per the previous procedures.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                25


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 26 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and

    2. Jet in the 30” casing and once stable release the Drill ahead tool and
     commence drilling the 26” hole.
    3.  If the 30” is cemented run in with the 26” BHA. Re-enter the 30” housing
      using the DSC (Drill String Compensator) and ROV (Remote Operated
       Vehicle).
    4. Continue running in and tag cement.  Drill out the shoe, clean out the
       rathole and new formation with reduced parameters until the stabilizers are
        in new hole.
    5. Increase the parameters and continue to drill to casing depth.

Note:  Refer  also  to  well  control  procedure  for  specific  precautions and
Instructions to follow while drilling.

Operation:

    1. A Rig Specific Procedure for handling the Master Bushings during riserless
       conditions, running surface casing, BOP and riser operations will be in
      place and  followed  (4.2.8.2 –  Pulling the Master Bushings while
       Riserless).
    2. A hole cover will be in place when ongoing activities associated with the
      removal and replacement of rotary table components are suspended for
      even the shortest period of time.
    3.  It is recommended that a pilot hole is drilled adjacent to the location prior to
      moving onto location and spudding the well.
    4. Shallow gas procedures will be implemented for this section as detailed in
      the well control manual.
    5. Jet in the 30” casing and once stable release the Drill ahead tool and
     commence drilling the 26” hole. Refer to procedure 4.1.4 - Run and Jet
      30” Conductor Pipe.
    6. Flow rates should be high using seawater with viscous sweeps as required
       to clean the hole.
    7.  If the hole cleaning does become a problem, then increase the frequency
       of the sweeps or the volume of the sweeps.
    8. Once any stabilizers in the string are past the shoe and in the new hole,
      then the R.P.M may be increased.
    9.  Drill to the casing point plus the rat hole required, normally 5-6m.
   10. Take any directional surveys as per the drilling program.
   11. At the casing point displace the hole to viscous mud and make a wiper trip
       to the surface conductor shoe.
   12. It is recommended that the compensator is used when any stabilizers and
      the bit enter the shoe.
   13. If bridges or excessive  fill are noticed, then repeat the hole displacement
     and wiper trips until the hole  is in an acceptable condition to run the
       casing.
   14. When the hole is in a satisfactory condition displace  it with viscous mud
     and pull out to run the casing.

   15. It may be necessary to displace the hole with weighted viscous mud to
      keep it open.
   16. Pull out of the hole to the drill ahead tool. J into the tool and un-J out of the
      30” housing.
   17. Continue to pull out the hole until the bit is just inside the 30” housing, and
       flush the wellhead housing at maximum pump rate using seawater.
   18. Do not allow the bit to contact the top of the well head housing.
   19. Stand back as much of the bottom hole assembly as possible and do not
      break off the bit.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clean up drill floor
    2. Ensure all stabilisers are clean before racking back
    3. Rig up the wireline for logging , if required
    4. Prepare casing equipment as per procedure 4.1.6 – Running and
     Cementing 20” – 185/8” Casing.
    5. Monitor well when out of hole
    6.  Slip and cut drill line if required
    7. Review the procedure and JSA for any improvement opportunities.

4.1.6  Running and Cementing 20” - 185/8” Casing

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
running and cementing the 20” casing.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the running and cementing of the 20” casing.

Responsible Persons:

Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  To ensure that the operations are carried out in accordance with the
      defined operations programme and are conducted in a safe and efficient
      manner.
   •  Supervise operations in the moon pool.
   •  Confirm casing  tallies, running order and cementing programme are
       correct.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of operations.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Assist as required with moonpool activities.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure tallies and depth measurements are accurate.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.



Crane Operator / Deck Crew:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                28


────────────────────────────────────────────

                         Issue Date

   •  Supervise and conduct all deck operations associated with this process.
   •  Support all rig floor and moon pool activities as required.
   •  Bring to the attention of the on tour Toolpusher any occurrence that may
       affect the safe and smooth efficient running of operations.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3.  Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc).
    4. PPE to be worn all the times. Chin straps to be worn on the drill floor.
    5.  All non essential personnel to be kept clear of Drill floor & Casing Bay.
    6. Ensure weather conditions are within the working range of the rig.
    7. Yellow & Red Alarms to be tested.
    8. Ear protection to be worn in high noise areas.

Preparation Prior to Running Casing:

Consider the following items when preparing to install 20” casing:

    1. Read through and check Operator’s casing program.
    2. Number, measure, and record lengths of the following items:
          •  Casing shoe
          •  Casing joints
          •  Squinch joint crossovers
          •  Wellhead housing
          •  Wellhead housing running tool.
    3. Measure and record casing; number casing and plan sequence.
    4. Clean,  drift and inspect each individual  joint. Ensure any  drifts used
      conform to API STD 5CT.
    5.  Drift the landing string.
    6.  Install centralizers as per the agreed program.
    7. Ensure that shoe and first joint connections are cleaned and covered ready
        for thread locking if required.
    8. Check that protectors are removed and that pin and boxes are cleaned.
    9. Check shoe and float mechanisms and that shoe, float, and first collars are
       thread-locked. (Check for debris in shoe joints).
   10. Operator Drilling Supervisor to prepare casing running list to be approved
      by the Toolpusher and distributed to driller, deck crew, crane operator,
      cementer and casing crew.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                29

   11. Paint shoe white and paint 1m marker rings over the first 33 feet (10 m)
      from shoe.
   12. Paint marker rings at 3.3-foot 1m intervals over last wellhead joint from top
       of wellhead.
   13. Run test the power casing tong.
   14. Casing handling equipment and cement stinger handling equipment to be
      prepared & inspected (side door elevator, single joint elevator, casing slip,
       rotary bushing, bushing 20”, C  plate, Teflon  bull nose, casing   fill up
      system, etc).
   15. Check the elevators will fit the casing to be run.
   16. Cement stinger, drifted and measured.
   17. Dimensional and visual check on wellhead housing and running tool.
   18. Make up the wellhead running tool on cement stinger pup adaptor and
     make up this assembly to the wellhead housing for handling purposes –
      rack back or lay out on pipe deck.
   19. Refer also to 20” casing running / cementing general practices.
   20. Circulating swedge, check same as casing thread.
   21. Dog collar clamp adjusted to size.
   22. Thread lock.
   23. Casing dope and brushes.
   24. Rig casing tongs set for casing to be run with long snub line for use as a
      back up tong.
   25. Back-up casing tongs.
   26. Casing fill up line.
   27. Cement hose hooked up to cement line and standpipe.
   28. Side entry sub made up in a stand with an IBOP above (to isolate the top
       drive if required).
   29. Stinger and landing string measurements.
   30. Manual drill pipe elevators.
   31. Hand slips.
   32. Mud tanks to fill casing.
   33. Mud tanks containing pre-flush or mix water.
   34. Mud balance and cups in cement room.
   35. Cement silos pressured up, valves checked, and vent line cleared.
   36. Ensure surge tank line is clear.
   37. Ensure the back pressure valve operational.
   38. Record pit volumes.
   39. Reset pump stroke counter to zero.


Running 20” Casing:

    1. Move vessel 20 to 30 m. from location.
    2. Prepare shoe joint on catwalk with crane and sling 1m above shoe and drill
        floor air winch 2m from collar. The catwalk tugger should be attached with
     a sling just above the shoe.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                30


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 31 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

3. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
   catwalk winch driver and deck foreman hoist the shoe joint to the drill floor
   keeping it slightly below horizontal to prevent it swinging into the drill floor
   when it reaches the top of the V door ramp.
4. Slowly bring the joint into the drill floor and land on the deck. Remove the
   protector and prepare the box as per the Operator Drilling Supervisor
    instructions.
5.  Prior to lifting the shoe joint with the blocks remove the master bushings
   and install 20” rotary bushings.
6. Rotate the side door elevators to face the V door ramp and open them.
7. Pick up shoe joint with the drill floor winch and using the crane and catwalk
   winch guide the joint into the elevators. When in the correct position drop
   the joint firmly into the elevators to turn them vertical causing them to
   close. Check they are properly latched and install the retaining pin.
8. Remove the drill floor winch and sling.
9. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, driller catwalk
   winch driver and deck foreman hoist the shoe joint simultaneously slacking
    off on the catwalk winch and crane keeping the bottom of the pipe clear of
   the deck.
10. When near vertical, extend the tailing arm, slack off until the tailing arms
   rests against the trailing arm. Remove the crane and catwalk airwinch.
11. Once secure in the rotary, retract the tailing arm and lower the catwalk
   airwinch to the main deck using the V door winch.
12. Pump water into the shoe joint through the top drive observing in the
   moonpool to ensure the float shoe operates satisfactorily.
13. Set the shoe joint in the rotary and secure using a spare single joint
   elevator or safety clamp above the manual slips. The handles of which
   should be secured with rope for the first 5 joints.
14. Pick up the second joint as per steps 2-11. Keep it secure with the tailing
   arm.
15. When all is ready clean both the pin and box and apply thread locking
   compound to the last third of the pin end thread (If threaded connections).
16. If quick connections are used clean and oil the pin and box and ensure the
   o ring is in place prior to making up. Install the locking device as per the
   operator drilling supervisor instructions.
17. Stab the joint onto the joint in the rotary and retract the tailing arm. Make
   up the joint as per the operator instructions.
18. Continue running the casing applying locking compound as required.
19. Continue running the 20” casing as per the tally filling each joint with sea
   water. Install casing centralizers as per program (if not done on the deck).
20. Confirm that the correct number of joints has been run and the correct
   number remain on deck.
21. Pick up and make up the wellhead housing assembly to the last joint of
   casing. Install the false table and land wellhead housing on the rotary.
   (Record the total weight before landing).
22. Release and rack back the wellhead RT in the derrick.

                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                31


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 32 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date

   23. Install the plastic protector on top of the wellhead housing.
   24. Rig up handling equipment and run cement stinger inside the casing using
     a keyhole plate or false rotary.
   25. The space out is to be adjusted to have the bottom of the stinger + / - 20
      meters above the shoe or float collar.
   26. Make up the wellhead housing RT assembly on the cement stinger and
      then make up the RT to the wellhead housing.
   27. Ensure that the running tool does not back out while RIH. Paint a vertical
      white mark on the RT and housing as an indicator.
   28. Ensuring that the RT 2” air bleed valve is fully open run the wellhead
      housing down to sea level.
   29. Connect the top drive, fill up the casing string and close the 2” valve.

Post Task Actions:

    1.  Prior to picking up the hanger and landing string, count the number of
        joints left on the deck. Verify the correct number have been run.
    2. Change over to drill pipe handling equipment.
    3. Clear the drill floor of any equipment no longer required.
    4. Clean up the drill floor.

Run 20” Casing on Landing String

    1. Run the casing on the landing string until just above WHD depth, filling up
      the string on each stand.
    2. Move the vessel back to well centre and with ROV assistance, re-enter the
       well (open the DSC for entering the 30” housing).
    3. When running  large diameter  strings  or casing  great care must be
      exercised not to be to aggressive  if the hole is tight. Typically the larger
       sizes of casing are equipped with quick connect couplings which will not
      withstand rough treatment.
    4. Space out to have the side entry sub within 10m of the drill floor while
      cementing. Pick up the cementing stand, make it up to the string and then
        install a low torque valve and the cementing hose secured with safety
      wires as required.
    5.  Verify the low torque valve is closed and the IBOP is open.
    6. Open and adjust the DSC to mid stroke.
    7.  Start circulating and continue to run in, land and latch the wellhead into the
      30” housing observing with the ROV if visibility allows.
    8. Stop pumping and check the wellhead inclination and perform a 15 to 20
      tonnes pick up test using the DSC.
    9.  Circulate at least 120% of casing volume + string.

Post Task Actions:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                32


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 33 از 286 ]

    1. During the  circulation, inform the Marine Department  to  fluff up and
       pressurize the Cement silos.
    2. Ensure the cementer is ready.
    3. Mud balance and cups in cement room.
    4. Ensure surge tank line is clear.
    5. Communication system between cement unit and driller to be tested.
    6. Mud pits and lines cleaned and flushed ready for mixing spacers or cement
      mix water.
    7. Transfer lines from mud pits to cement unit cleaned and flushed.
    8. Mix the cement mix water.
    9. Mix the spacer when requested (ensure no loss or contamination).
   10. Record pit volumes.

Cementing 20” Casing

Preparation prior to cement job:

    1. Double check calculations with cementing engineer and operator.
    2. Advise DP room and engine room that the job is about to start.  Double
      check weather forecast.
    3. Ensure mechanic and electrician are standing in case of emergency.
    4. The following actions to be taken by the Derrick Man / Mud Supervisor and
      double checked by the Assistant Driller: Mud pits and lines cleaned and
       flushed ready for mixing spacers or cement mix water.
    5. Mix the cement mix water.
    6. Mix the spacer when requested (ensure no loss or contamination)
    7. Cement lines blown through.
    8. Pressurize the cement bulk tanks with the first tank to be used properly
       lined up and fluffed.
    9. The cement job should not start on a part full tank.
   10. The surge tank ready, standing by with supply valve and vent open.
   11. Mix water ready and lined up to cementing unit.
   12. Ensure rig floor cement manifold and cement lines have been flushed and
      ready for use.
   13. Communication system between cement unit and DMC to be tested.
   14. Transfer lines from mud pits to cement unit cleaned and flushed.

Cement Job:

    1. Flush and pressure test the lo torque valve, cementing hose and surface
       lines to 5000psi (or as Operator Drilling Supervisor instructions).
    2. Line up the spacer and transfer to the cement unit as required.
    3. Line up to transfer the mix water to the cement unit and also to circulate on
         itself to prevent deadheading of the pump.
    4. Mix and pump cement slurry as per the program.
    5. Monitor the returns at the wellhead with the ROV throughout the job.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                33


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 34 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    6. Take weights and samples as required by the cementer.
    7. Displace surface lines with the cementing unit and continue displacement
      using rig pumps.
    8. When the desired strokes have been pumped release the pressure
      watching for returns.
    9.  If the float is not holding, then pump the volume bled back and hold the
       shut-in pressure until the cement takes its initial set.
   10. Rig down the cement hose and lo-torque valve.
   11. Observing with the ROV, disengage the wellhead running tool from the
      wellhead, as per the Wellhead Service Engineer instruction.
   12. Pull out of the hole and position the end of stinger 3-5m inside the top of
      the wellhead. It is critical that running tool/stinger should be pulled cleanly
       to avoid damage to wellhead gasket profile area.
   13. Flush the wellhead area with seawater at a maximum pump rate.
   14. Ensure the slips are not set when the stinger is near the wellhead and do
      not allow the stinger to contact the top of the wellhead.
   15. Pull out and lay down running tool and cement stinger.
   16. Strap  the  landing  string (SLM) on  the  trip  out and  calculate  the
     measurement from the rotary table to the top of wellhead housing.
   17. Verify that the ship is at normal drilling draft.
   18. Apply tide corrections and report this depth in the IADC report.
   19. Prepare to run the 18 ¾” BOPs.

Post Task Action:

    1. Flush the cement lines clear of slurry.
    2. Purge the bulk transfer lines to the surge tank.
    3. Vent off the bulk tanks.
    4. Service the mud pump fluid ends and change piston and liner sizes as
       required
    5. Clean high and low pressure mud pump strainers.
    6. Check the remaining stock of cement.
    7. Ensure that the surge tank is empty and made ready for the next job.
    8. Clean out the tanks used for spacer and mix water.
    9. Flush the mud mix lines.

Cement Calculations:

    1. Cement required (MT) = Slurry required (ltrs) / Yield (ltrs/MT).
    2. Mix Water required (M3) = Cement required (MT) x Mix Water  ratio
      (M3/MT).

Displacement:

    1. Volume of Landing String: Capacity of DP (ltrs/m) x Length of String (m) +
      Capacity of Stinger (ltrs/m) x Length of Stinger (m).

2. Displacement: Volume String + Volume Stinger + safety margin.

4.1.7 Running the BOP:

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation when
running the BOP stack on Riser.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the required  activities, which need to be
carried out prior and during the running of the BOP stack on Riser.

Responsible Persons:

Toolpusher / Subsea Engineer / Driller / Crane Operator / Deck Foreman

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparations:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Acquire weather forecasts and confirm a suitable weather window  is
       available for the operation.
    3. The rig is to be moved 50m off location while running the BOP.
    4. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    5. The clear-call system between the rig floor and the moonpool is to be
       operational before the job begins.
    6. Work vests (correctly donned and secured) must be worn when working in
       or near the side of the moonpool.
    7. PPE worn at all times.
    8. Safety harnesses attached to fall arrestors must be worn when working
      from the spider beams or from heights.
    9. Pin protectors must be correctly secured before any riser is removed from
      the catwalk.
   10. Never run an air winch down through the rotary to the moonpool for man
        riding.
   11. All air hose connections must be secured with "R" clips and whipchecks
   12. Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc).
   13. All non essential personnel to keep clear of the drill floor, raised catwalk
     and riser storage areas.
   14. Stand clear in the moon pool when the riser is moving through the rotary.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                36


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 37 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                        Issue Date
                                                         Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                         January 2011
                        Revision No.
                                           Manual Nr:
                              0
                        Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                         Document Title:
                                                                    Procedures Manual
                      Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                      K.Tiersma                          Approved by

  15. Ensure good communication between driller, POD reel operator and the
     crew working in moon pool.
  16. The BOP stack should not be run  if vessel motion is outside operating
      limits for BOP handling, or weather window is not sufficient to run and land
     the BOPs. Crane limitations are a maximum of 40 knots wind speed and
    maximum of 1.5 degrees (full amplitude) pitch and roll for handling the slip
       joint and landing joint.
  17. The riser tensioners are numbered 1 to 10 clockwise from Port Forward.
  18. Serial numbers of the riser and the position of each joint of riser run will be
     recorded while running so that the riser record book can be updated.
  19. Record the string weight after each joint is run to assess the buoyancy
      condition.
  20. Ensure there is no build up of scale or loose debris in the riser prior to
     running

Equipment Required:

  1. 500 ton bails and elevators
  2. Riser gantry crane
  3. Riser spider & operating remote pane
  4. Riser gimbal
  5. Riser trolley forward and aft. Run test the aft trolley
  6. Riser handling tools (Cameron RD and Vetco MR6E)
  7. Riser test tool (Cameron RD and Vetco MR6E)
  8. Water hose
  9. BOP gantry crane
  10. Drill floor tailing arm tested
  11. Spider beams
  12. Skidding system
  13. Diverter handling tool
  14. Load ring handling tool to install load ring on diverter housing
  15. Riser tally to be available for Tool Pusher, Driller and Crane operator
  16. Fall arrestors and safety harnesses
  17. New wellhead VX ring gasket
  18. New riser connector AX ring gasket
  19. Rags
  20. Pipe wrenches, crescent wrenches
  21. Shackles with spare retaining rings/pins and spare slings
  22. Allen wrenches
  23. Pneumatic impact wrenches  with pressure  regulators  set below  the
     required torque of the riser bolts
  24. Torque wrenches set for the required torque of the riser bolts (Cameron
   RD 250-300ft/lbs, Vetco MR6E 900-1000ft/lbs)
  25. Lock blocks and retainers for the kill, choke and booster line goosenecks
  26. POD and conduit hose clamps and tools (2) to tighten up the clamps
  27. Boat hook

BOP / LMRP preparation to be carried out between wells:

During previous drilling phase the following checks to be done prior to running the
BOP:

    1. Open all the ram bonnets and clean out the cavities.
    2. Check wear of the bonnet, seal face and main body of rams.
    3. Check for damage/wear of the ram piston rods and seals.
    4. Check the ram rubbers for damage and change as required.
    5. Pressure test  all the BOP hydraulic actuators to 250psi and maximum
      operating pressure except the annular close functions which will only be
       tested with pipe in them to 250psi and 1500 psi.
    6. Change the bonnet gaskets. Oil the bonnet faces and close them. Torque
      the bolts to 6100ft/lbs.
    7. Visual checks on the annular preventers; change the elements as required.
    8. Adjust the pre-charge pressure on the sub sea accumulators including the
      annular surge bottles.
    9. Check the pre-charge pressure on the surface accumulators’.
   10. Perform function test from both yellow & blue pods.
   11. Function test the wellhead and LMRP connectors and the kill and choke
       mini connectors.
   12. Operate the rams with 500 psi and check for ease of movement.
   13. Check the three (3) electrically operated pumps on the BOP control unit
       (calibration and auto start).
   14. Visually check the BOP and LMRP wellbores for wear or key seating.
   15. Pick up the BOP and LMRP with the BOP crane in turn. Clean and grease
      the connectors. Clean the ring grooves and install new gaskets on the
    LMRP and BOP skid test stumps.
   16. Install the test plugs and pressure test the BOPs. Rams and fail safes to
      250psi for 5mins and 10,000psi for 10mins. Annulars to 250psi for 5mins
     and 3500 (70% of SWP) for 10mins.
   17. Remove the test plugs.
   18. Check that all tools and equipment have been removed from the BOP.

                                   16 ¾” BOP Stack
                              Dry weight (lbs)   Wet weight (lbs)
            LMRP          142 992         124 120
            BOP           236 005         204 854
               Total weight       378 997         328 974

         1. Check the seal condition on  all connections (riser,  kill, and choke and
           booster line).
         2. Check the riser pins and boxes for wear or damage.
         3. Check the wellbore of the riser pins and boxes for wear or key seating.
         4. Check riser buoyancy modules for cracks or damage. Ensure the retaining
            straps are tight and in good condition.

           clamps.

              total duration of the well - pressure test the same.

            table to the 16 3/4" wellhead measurement at drilling draft and adjusted for

             of 47.06m is found as follows:

              BOP height                       13.41m

                 Top of Diverter to bottom of ball joint     2.5m
                 Top adapter Slip joint                 0.4m
                     Inner Barrel (Mid stroke)             8.35m
                      Total                            47.06m

        11. The slick joints and buoyant riser are stored in the aft deck riser bays. The
