4.1.10  Drilling 171/2” – 143/4” Hole


Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure a smooth and safe operation while
drilling 14 ¾”- 17 ½”section.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the activities that are to be carried out prior to
and during the drilling of 14 ¾”-17 ½”” hole section.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  Ensure  that the  drilling and associated operations are carried out  in
      accordance with the defined operations programme and are conducted in
     a safe and efficient manner.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of the Drilling and associated operations.
   •  Ensure that the unit has sufficient resources and supplies to perform
        drilling and associated activities as per contractual requirements.
   •  Confirm the calculations made by the driller.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.
   •  Measure and tally the next casing string.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this  process.
   •  Ensure that accurate tallies and depth control is maintained.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s representative if a deviation
      from the planned operations occurs.

Assistant Driller:

   •  Maintain pipe tally, drilling tally and BHA records accurately and up to date.
   •  Supervise and assist with drill floor, pump/pit room, shaker house, mixing
     and fluid conditioning operations.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                66


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 67 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   •  Pre-plan and make ready the correct tools, equipment and necessary
     manpower for forthcoming operations.
   •  Ensure that the Driller’s instructions to crewmembers are carried out.
   •  Bring to the attention of the Driller anything that may affect the safe and
     smooth running of the operation.
   •  Assist third party personnel involved in drilling operations.
   •  Check, measure, document and inspect tubulars and drilling tools to be run
        in the hole.
   •  Assist with deck operations when required.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Pressure test all BOPs and associated well control equipment.
   •  Responsible for running and setting the nominal bore protector.

Deck Crew:

   •  Transfer of equipment to and from the drill floor in the correct sequence as
       required.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations
    3. Pre-Job Meeting held with all the parties involved
    4.  Lifting gear inspected
    5. PPE worn at all times
    6. Weather conditions in the working range of the vessel
    7. Yellow & Red Alarms tested
    8. Well control equipment tested and line up for drilling
    9. Back up safety valves ready for use on rig floor
   10. Kill sheet updated
   11. Kill mud available
   12. Non shearable procedure posted in the DCR.
   13. Drilling program available and instructions given in writing to the driller.
   14. Mud characteristics and mud volume ready as per program.
   15. Casing pressure tested.
   16. Bore protector installed in the wellhead.
   17. All Bottom Hole Assembly components measured with  all dimensions
      recorded on drawings and ready for picking up. This includes drill bit and
       nozzles.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                67


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 68 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

General:

    1. The minimum quantity of barite (or any alternative weighting material) held
     on board will be sufficient to raise the density of the mud system by at least
      1ppg, where:
             •  Mud system = hole volume (no pipe) + 150 BBLS surface
               volume.
    2. The minimum quantities of bulk cement and cement additives held on
      board will be sufficient to set at least two (2) 500ft cement plugs in the hole
       section being drilled.
    3.  Drilling operations will be stopped if minimum stock levels are reached.
    4. Pressure test the BOPs and  all associated well control equipment as
      defined in the well control manual.
    5.  Install the 16¾" nominal bore protector in the wellhead and rack the
       running/pulling tool in the derrick. CMC (Crown Mounted compensator) to
     be used when pulling full bore tools through the BOP and slip joint shoe.
    6. With the drill pipe in the hole, fully function test the diverter system. Pump
      sea water at a maximum rate through both overboard lines.
    7. On some well head systems it is only possible to hang off in the well head
       after the 13 ⅜" casing hanger has been set.  In these cases a ram type
     hang off tool should be used for the 14 ¾" hole section.
    8. Make up the emergency drill pipe hang off tool assembly as required and
      rack it back in the derrick.
    9. Lay out the 26" bottom hole assembly.
   10. Make up and rack the 13⅜" casing hanger and pup joint assembly to the
       pack-off assembly, running tool and sub sea plug launching system.
   11. Make up and rack the cementing and dart-launching head to a stand of
        drifted landing string.
   12. Verify that the float equipment is PDC drillable  if  it is intended to use a
    PDC bit.

Operation:

    1. Pick up and rack at least enough drill pipe to complete the next section.
    2. Make up the 14 ¾” x 17½" bottom hole assembly (refer to procedures
       4.2.1.3 – Picking Up and Laying Out Tubulars above 61/2”OD, 4.2.1.7 –
      Tripping Drill Collars 8” OD and below, 4.2.1.8 – Tripping 91/2” Drill
       Collars).
    3. Run in the hole with  drill pipe until the 17½" bottom hole assembly to
      approximately 6m above the predicted top of cement.
    4. The CMC (Crown Mounted Compensator) will be open while the bit passes
      through the BOP/wellhead space out so the bit is not in the BOPs while
       picking up the next stand.
    5. Inform DP when BHA passing through the BOP.
    6. With no rotation, break circulation slowly and tag the cement to check for
       firmness.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                68


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 69 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

7. Note and record the depth of the cement and inform the Toolpusher and
   Operator Drilling Supervisor of any major discrepancy.
8. Float equipment should be drilled with seawater at high flow rates and
  WOB with low RPM.
9.  Drill out the cement and the shoe. Note the depth of the shoe and inform
   Toolpusher and Operator Drilling Supervisor of any major discrepancy.
10. Clean out the 26" rat hole as the program dictates then displace the hole to
  new drilling mud. Take care to avoid cement contamination.
11. Where the BOPs are to be used for well control, as a minimum a
   formation  integrity  test should be conducted followed by slow
   circulation rates and choke line friction values.
12. Once the stabilizers and under reamer are out of the shoe and in new hole
   then the rotary speed may be increased. Parameters will be at Operator
    Drilling Supervisor instruction.
13. Take any directional surveys as required by the programme.
14. The riser may be boosted if the cuttings load is too great.
15. Take slow circulating rates at the start of each shift and when the mud
   properties are changed.
16. Choke  line  friction values should be taken when the mud properties
   change.
17. Drill to the casing point plus the rat hole required, normally 5-6m. The
   height of the under reamer blades above the bit to be taken into account.
18. Prepare the 13⅜" casing.
19. Inspect the bore protector and pressure test the BOPs at intervals
   specified in the well programme and the Well Control Manual.
20. At the casing point, circulate the hole clean and make a wiper trip to the
   casing shoe.
21. If bridges or excessive fill are encountered, then repeat the wiper trips until
   the hole is in an acceptable condition to run open hole electric logs or the
   casing (refer to procedures 4.2.1.7 – Tripping Drill Collars 8” OD and
   below, 4.2.1.8 – Tripping 91/2” Drill Collars).
22. Flow checks are to be performed when the bit is at the lowest casing shoe
   and prior to the BHA being pulled into the BOP. Extra care to be taken
   when the under reamer, stabilizers and bit enter the shoe, and remove the
    diverter packer before pulling the BHA.
23. It may be required to perform an SLM (steel line measure) while pulling out
    of the hole and report any difference in the drilled depth.
24. If open hole logs are carried out, then a wiper trip may be required before
   running the casing.
25. Circulate and condition the mud ready to run the casing.
26. The wellhead should not be washed with the drilling BHA because of the
   under reamer. A jet sub should be run  if required or the BOP flushed
   through the choke and kill lines.

   27. Retrieve and inspect the 16¾" nominal bore protector. Details regarding
      any wear as well as wear orientation will be recorded in the IADC report.
     Do not allow the string to rotate while recovering the wear bushing.
   28. Rack back or lay out the wear bushing running retrieving tool.
   29. Prepare to run the 13 ⅜” casing.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clean up drill floor
    2. Ensure all stabilisers are clean after racking back
    3. Rig up the wireline for logging , if required
    4. Prepare casing equipment as per procedure 4.1.11 – Running and
     Cementing 133/8” Casing.
    5. Monitor well when out of hole
    6.  Slip and cut drill line if required
    7. Review the procedure and JSA for any improvement opportunities.

4.1.11     Running and Cementing 133/8” Casing

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
running and cementing the 13 ⅜” casing.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the running and cementing of the 13 ⅜” casing.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  To ensure that the operations are carried out in accordance with the
      defined operations programme and are conducted in a safe and efficient
      manner.
   •  Supervise operations in the moon pool.
   •  Confirm that any calculations made are correct.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of operations.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Assist as required with moonpool activities.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure tallies and depth measurements are accurate.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.

Crane Operator / Deck Crew:

   •  Supervise and conduct all deck operations associated with this process.
   •  Support all rig floor and moon pool activities as required.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                71


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 72 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011

   •  Bring to the attention of the on tour Toolpusher any occurrence that may
       affect the safe and smooth efficient running of operations.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3. Pre-Job Meeting to be held with all the parties involved.
    4.  Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc).
    5. PPE is to be worn all the times. Chin straps to be worn on the drill floor.
    6.  All non essential personnel to be kept clear of Drill floor & Casing Bay.
    7. Ensure weather conditions are within the working range of the vessel.
    8. Yellow & Red Alarms to be tested.
    9. Ear protection to be worn in high noise areas.

Preparation Prior to Running Casing:

Consider the following items when preparing to run 13 3/8” casing:

    1. Read through and check Operator’s casing program.
    2. The casing shall be laid out on the pipe deck; a working bay should be
      kept beside the cat walk. An accurate count of all casing received will be
     made.
    3. Check that protectors are removed and that pin and boxes are cleaned.
    4.  All the Casing will be drifted, inspected, measured and an accurate tally
     made. The drift will conform to API Spec 5CT.
    5. Lengths and joint numbers will be painted on the casing if not done ashore.
      This to include the pup joints but with letters (A, B, C etc.).
    6. Any joints not to be run, rejected or which could not be drifted will be
       clearly marked and easily identifiable and recorded on the casing tally.
    7. The threads on shoe track joint,  will be thoroughly cleaned, dried and
       “black bagged”. Shoe track joints shall be checked for debris, prior to being
      picked up.
    8. Check that the collars on the shoe track joints, the float and the shoe are
         all threadlocked.
    9. Stop collars and centralizers can be fitted on deck, where possible.
   10. All lifting equipment will have current certification and colour coding. It will
     be inspected and made ready at the work site, with tag lines.
   11. Casing  handling equipment  to be prepared &  inspected  (side door
       elevator, single joint elevator, casing slip, rotary bushing inserts, casing fill
     up system etc). Test fit the elevators on all the grades of casing to be run.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                72


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 73 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir

12. Single joint elevators will be connected to the v-door air winch on the rig
    floor and correct size confirmed by the asst driller.
13. Dog collar clamp adjusted to size and hand slips with rope for securing the
   handles.
14. Back-up casing tongs with long snub line set to casing size.
15. “Klamp on” air operated protectors shall be checked and made ready at
   the work site.  Dedicated airlines and attachments will be secured with
   whip checks.
16. A rope and hook will be hung on the v door ramp for returning the “Klamp
   ons” to the catwalk.
17. FMS (Flush Mounted Slips), if used, dressed with the correct size inserts,
   guides, will be lubricated, function tested and slung ready for picking up.
18. The Operator Drilling Supervisor will prepare a casing running list for the
    Driller.  This  will have been checked and  approved by  the  Senior
   Toolpusher. The joints will be marked where they enter the BOP and open
   hole. Distribute same to driller, deck crew and crane operator, cementer
   and casing crew when approved.
19. While POOH, strap the pipe (SLM (Steel Line Measure)) if required by the
   Operator Drilling Supervisor.  Result to be communicated to the Senior
   Tool pusher.
20. Wash and jet wash the wellhead area as per operational requirements.
21. Recover the wear bushing. To get an accurate measurement the Landing
   String should be used along with a Datum line, where possible.
22. Drift the landing string.
23. Clear the Drill Floor of any excess equipment not required for the casing
    job.
24. Stabbing Board to be checked, as per the check list, and lubricated. The
   Casing crew  shall be given  instructions  in  its use. To include  clear
    instructions on the hand signals required for the driller when the board flap
    is retracted.
25. A fall arrestor is to be installed above the Stabbing board prior to use.
26. Rig up to run casing using side door elevators.
27. If FMS are to be used they can be run from the start.
28. Casing dope, barite and thread cleaning fluid, pressure washer, thread
   locking compound and rags will be required.
29. Centralizers, stop collars, nails, hand tools and goggles if centralizers not
   previously installed.
30. Run test the power casing tong. Any time the power tong has to be laid
   down or not in use, the hydraulic power unit will be turned off and the
   pressure bled off. The tong will be functioned to verify status.
31. All handling gear to have current certification and be within limitations of
   job requirement.
32. All slings and shackles will have current certification, colour coding and
   inspected prior to use.  All shackles will be of 4 parts, ensure retaining
   rings/pins installed.
33. Record all pit volumes. Inform the logging unit of line up and operation.

                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                73


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 74 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011

                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:

   34. Circulating swedge, check same as casing thread.
   35. Ensure thread compatible stabbing valve is made up. All crew members
         will be instructed in its use and the location.
   36. Space out of the Landing string calculated to give a workable stick up
      above the rotary table.
   37. Remove the rotary inserts and Master bushings and install the FMS slips if
      they are to be used. Install hole cover.
   38. A Pre-job Safety meeting with all personnel involved will be held prior to
       rigging up. These will also be held at shift changes. If the job changes at
      any time, for any reason, a meeting should be held to discuss the way
       forward. Remind all crew members to look up at all times, and ensure the
      stabbing board operators are fully aware of signals needed for the driller to
      confirm the elevator status and to confirm that the flap is retracted.
   39. Establish communications with the Stabber and check on the signals to be
      used.
   40. Clean the Derrick camera as required.
   41. Review and discuss the procedure and JSA for the job.
   42. The crews are to be made aware that casing will continually be brought
       into the drill floor and as such they should not work with their backs to the
    V door.
   43. All personnel working on the drill floor will wear chinstraps.
   44. Pressure test the cement hose and Lo-Torque valve assembly as per the
     cement program. This may be done off line.
   45. Plugs, darts installed in the cement head and plug launcher. This should
     be done  off the  critical path where possible. Personnel involved  for
        installing plugs: Toolpusher, Operator Drilling Supervisor, Cementer and
      Wellhead Engineer.
   46. Cement Head made up and racked in the Derrick, one single up.
   47. The Hanger, running tool and sub sea launcher to be made up and racked
        in  the  Derrick.   If  required  the pack  off assembly can be  installed
      immediately prior to running.
   48. Dimensional and visual checks on the casing hanger and running tool.
   49. Prepare Mix water and Spacers as per the cement program. Be aware of
     dead volume. Check pit space for displacement. Possibly need a boat
       alongside.
   50. Check all lifting gear; ensure the rings are installed in the shackles.
   51. Install the side door elevators and rig up a tailing in rope.
   52. The safe and smooth running of a casing string depends upon safety
      awareness, good teamwork and excellent communication between  all
      personnel involved in the operation.

Running 13 ⅜” Casing:

    1. Prepare the shoe joint on the catwalk with crane and sling 1m above the
      shoe and drill floor air winch and sling 2m below the collar.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                74


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 75 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

2. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
   and deck foreman, hoist the shoe joint to the drill floor keeping  it slightly
   below horizontal to prevent  it swinging into the drill floor when  it reaches
   the top of the V door ramp.
3. Slowly bring the joint into the drill floor and land on the deck. Remove the
   protector and prepare the box as per the Operator Drilling Supervisor
    instructions for thread locking.
4.  Prior to lifting the shoe joint with the blocks install the #1 bushings inserts
   and install a hole cover.
5. Rotate the side door elevators to face the V door ramp and open them.
6. Pick up shoe joint with the drill floor winch and using the crane guide the
    joint into the elevators. When in the correct position drop the joint firmly
    into the elevators to turn them vertical causing them to close. Check they
   are properly latched and install the retaining pin.
7. Slack off and remove the drill floor winch and sling.
8. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator and deck
   foreman, hoist the shoe joint simultaneously slacking off on the catwalk
   winch and crane keeping the float shoe clear of the deck.
9. When near vertical take the weight with the tailing rope, slack off the crane
   and remove the sling.
10. During Steps 11 through 16, the crane can place the next joints onto the
   pipe deck. All personnel to stay well clear of the joint as it is being moved
    into position and landed, tag lines must be used.
11. Swing the shoe in to well centre and run in hole. Set the slips and fill it with
   mud, then pick up far enough to observe the mud draining out of the shoe.
12. Run the joint back in and set the slips. Check the level of the mud, to see if
      it has dropped. Secure the slip handles with rope (first 5 joints) and install
   a single joint elevator or safety clamp above the slips.
13. If using flush mounted slips these should be used from the start and no
   dog collar  is required. Only the casing crew  will operate the FMS on
    instructions from the man on the brake.
14. Unlatch the elevators.
15. Mark off on the casing tally all the joints as they are run. Record any
   deviation in the running order to the Toolpusher and the Client rep.
16. Pick up the next shoe track joint (intermediate) using steps 1 to 9, keeping
      it secure with the tailing in rope. Do not hold the casing with hands inside
   the pin or box connections.
17. When both joints ready clean the pin (be aware of steel splinters on the
   threads).and apply thread locking compound to the last third of the pin end
   thread.
18. Stab into the joint in the rotary table using the correct stabbing guide, slack
    off slightly on the elevators. The stabber will guide the casing from the
   stabbing board while it is spun in (unlatch the elevators if required). Do not
   hold the casing with hands inside the pin or box connections.



                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                75


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 76 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

19. Pick up or swing in the Power tong, spin in and make the connections up
    to the required torque/ mark as per the operator instructions using a back
   up tong and long snub line as required.
20. Remove the power and back up tongs and rack back or lower to the floor.
  Be aware of pinch points when using the tongs and use the correct
   handles.
21. When the joint has been torqued up, the Stabber will latch the sidedoor
   elevators and signal the Driller to confirm, and then signal again once the
   board extension is raised.
22. Remove the dog collar and pick up the weight, pull the slips and slowly run
    in the hole.
23. Continue running the shoe track  joints applying locking compound as
   required (typically 3 joints).
24. Record the pit volumes when starting and monitor the displacement while
   running casing.
25. Fill the casing with mud and pull back until the shoe is visible to check that
  mud passes through the float and shoe. RIH again and set the slips. Check
   the level of the mud; it should below the float collar.
26. Install the single joint elevators in a bridle below the side door elevators.
   Ensure that the safety pin is in position to prevent the elevators from
   unlatching.
27. Install a “Klamp on” protector on the pin end of the next joint and using a
     drill floor air winch fitted with a set of single joint elevators, bring the next
    joint into the Vee door ramp.
28. Set the joint onto the stepped ramp (if fitted) on the V door ramp. Slack off
   and remove the air winch. If the stepped ramp is not used then a sling will
   be used to lift and hold the joint until the pick up elevators on the top drive
   take the weight.
29. Remove the box end protector and dope the thread (this only to be done at
   the V door).
30. Latch the pick up elevators on the blocks onto the joint at the v door.
31. Pick up on the blocks and using tailing rope to control the joint, bring it into
   the floor. Keep it held back from the joint in the rotary table. Do not allow
   anyone to walk between the suspended joint and the one in the rotary or to
   walk under the joint as it is being picked up.
32. Remove the “Klamp-on” protectors with feet well out of the way and lower
   them down the V door with the rope and hook - do not throw them. Stab
    into the joint, in the Rotary table, remove the stabbing guide.
33. Slack off on the single joint elevators and clamp the casing tong onto the
    joint.
34. The man on the stabbing board will unlatch the pick ups and signal to
   lower the elevators depending type used.
35. The Stabber will rock the pipe to assist in getting the threads started. Once
   spun in the elevators, lower over the box until the elevators are below the
   box collar. Once torqued up, latch the elevators and install the retaining
    pin.

                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                76


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 77 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   36. Remove and secure the tongs. The Stabber will confirm that the elevators
      are latched and the stabbing board has been raised.
   37. Pick up and take the string weight pull the slips and RIH at controlled
      speed. Monitor the displacement. Check for losses or gains with the mud
       loggers.
   38. While making up the joints, the crane crew will be preparing the catwalk
       with more casing. A joint can be pulled into the V door.
   39. Set the  slips  (tying the handles and  installing the dog  collar/pick up
       elevators as required) latch the pick up elevators on the joint in the V door
      ensuring the retaining pin is installed correctly.
   40. When there is sufficient weight in the string the back up tong will not be
       required.
   41. Continue running the 13 ⅜ casing as per the tally filling every joint and
      completely filling  it every 5 joints. 'Listen' for the height of the mud in the
      casing from a distance not over the top of the joint.
   42. Monitor the string weight and  if the weight reaches 80% of the elevators
    SWL (safe working load), then change to the 500ton elevators and spiders.
   43. Check all the lifting gear on a regular basis.
   44. Confirm that the correct number of joints has been run and the correct
     number remains on deck.
   45. Continue to run casing as the above procedure, until the hanger is to be
      picked up. Pick up and make up the casing hanger assembly to the last
        joint of casing. Land the casing hanger in the rotary (record the total weight
      before landing).
   46. Release the running tool and install the pack off  if not already installed.
     Make up the running tool again. Check the running tool as required by the
      Service Engineer.
   47. Ensure the casing is full prior to running drill pipe.



Post Task Actions:

    1.  Prior to picking up the hanger and landing string, count the number of
        joints left on the deck. Verify the correct number has been run.
    2. Change over to drill pipe handling equipment.
    3. Clear the drill floor of any equipment no longer required.
    4. Clean up the drill floor.

Run 13 ⅜” Casing on Landing String:

    1. Run the casing on the landing string.  Fill up the string on each stand.
    2. To reduce surge pressures, open the kill and choke lines to surface while
      running in with the hanger.
    3. Open  the CMC (Crown Mounted Compensator) when the casing  is
       entering the BOP.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                77


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 78 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    4. Inform DP when casing entering BOP.
    5. Run the casing at controlled speed to prevent surge pressures. RIH at
      slow speed in open hole.
    6.  Prior to going into open hole, make up the top drive and break circulation.
      Record up and down weights.
    7. Work the pipe slowly through any ledges or tight spots and limit the set
     down weight. Inform Toolpusher and Operator Drilling Supervisor.
    8.  If unable to RIH make up the top drive and circulate down as required with
      low strokes. The Toolpusher must be on the drill floor.
    9. Monitor well closely for losses/gains.
   10. Before the casing hanger enters the BOP pick up the cementing and dart
      launching head and space out to have the cementing head within 10m of
      the drill floor while cementing. Install a lo-torque valve and cementing hose
      secured with safety wires as required.
   11. Verify the lo-torque valve and the cement head circulating valves are open.
   12. Open and adjust the CMC as required.
   13. Using the mud pumps and circulating through the cement manifold start
       circulating and continue to run in, and land the hanger in the wellhead
      housing.
   14. Verify the hanger has landed at the correct depth.
   15. Break circulation slowly with the rig pumps using drilling mud. Circulate at
       least the volume of the casing and landing string.
   16. Limit the pump rate so that the annular velocity is no greater than that used
       to drill the 143/4 hole.
   17. Observe for mud losses while circulating.



Post Task Actions:

    1. During the  circulation, inform the Marine Department  to  fluff up and
       pressurize the cement silos.
    2. Service the mud pump fluid ends and change piston and liner sizes as
       required
    3. Clean high and low pressure mud pump strainers.
    4. Ensure the cementer is ready.
    5. Mud balance and cups in cement room.
    6. Ensure surge tank line is clear.
    7. Communication system between cement unit and the driller to be tested.
    8. Mud pits and lines cleaned and flushed ready for mixing spacers or cement
      mix water.
    9. Transfer lines from mud pits to cement unit cleaned and flushed.
   10. Mix the cement mix water.
   11. Mix the spacer when requested (ensure no loss or contamination).
   12. Record pit volumes.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                78

Cementing 13 3/8 Casing:

Preparations prior to cement job:

    1. Double check calculations with cementing engineer and Operator Drilling
       Supervisor.
    2. Advise DP room and engine room that the job is about to start.  Double
      check weather forecast.
    3. Ensure mechanic and electrician are standing by in case of emergency.
    4. The following actions to be taken by the Derrick Man / Mud Supervisor and
      double checked by the Assistant Driller:
          •  Cement lines flushed and dried
          •  Bulk air system fully operational
          •  Ensure  rig  floor cement manifold and cement  lines have been
             flushed and ready for use.
    5. Mix water ready.
    6. Spacer lined up to cement unit.

Cement Job:

    1. Flush and pressure test the lo-torque valve, cementing hose and surface
       lines to 5000psi (or as per the cementing programme).
    2. Line up the spacer and transfer to the cement unit as required.
    3. Pump the spacer as cementing program.
    4. Line up to transfer the mix water to the cement unit and also to circulate on
         itself to prevent deadheading of the pump.
    5. Mix and pump cement slurry as per the program. Take weights and
      samples as required by the cementer.
    6. Monitor for lost circulation throughout the job.
    7. Release the top plug launching dart; displace the dart with the cement unit
       to the top subsea plug. Shear out the plug.
    8. Change over to the mud pumps and displace the top plug to the float collar
      using drilling mud.
    9. Do not over displace the cement by more than 50% of the shoe track
      volume.
   10. When the plug has bumped, pressure up the casing to the required test
      pressure as per the cement program. Hold the pressure for the specified
       time, record the volume required to pressure up the casing.
   11. Release the pressure, watching for returns.
   12. If the float is not holding, then pump the volume bled back and hold the
       shut-in pressure until the cement takes its initial set.
   13. Rig down the cement hose and lo-torque valve.
   14. Set the casing hanger seal assembly as per the wellhead service engineer
       instructions.
   15. Flush through the choke and kill lines and circulate the riser contents.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                79

[ صفحه 80 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir

   16. Close a set of pipe rams and pressure test the seal assembly as required
      by the drilling program.
   17. Verify that all surface lines have been flushed and are cement free.
   18. Disengage the wellhead running tool from the wellhead using the CMC.
   19. Pull out, inform DP when clear of BOP. (Use CMC when pulling full bore
       tools through the BOP and slip joint shoe).
   20. Lay down the running tool.
   21. Strap  the  landing  string (SLM) on  the  trip  out and  calculate  the
     measurement from the rotary table to the top of casing hanger if required.
   22. Verify that the ship is at normal drilling draft.
   23. Apply tide corrections and report this depth in the IADC report.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Flush the cement lines clear of slurry.
    2. Purge the bulk transfer lines to the surge tank.
    3. Vent off the bulk tanks.
    4. Check the remaining stock of cement.
    5. Ensure that the surge tank is empty and made ready for the next job.
    6. Clean out the tanks used for spacer and mix water.
    7. Flush the mud mix lines.

Cement calculations:

    1. Cement required (MT) = Slurry required (ltrs) / Yield (ltrs/MT).
    2. Mix Water required (M3) = Cement required (MT) x Mix Water  ratio
      (M3/MT).

Displacement :

    1. Volume of Landing String: Capacity of DP (ltrs/m) x Length of String (m) +
      Capacity of Stinger (ltrs/m) x Length of Stinger (m).
    2. Displacement: Volume String + Volume Stinger + safety margin.

4.1.12 Drilling 121/4” Hole

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure a smooth and safe operation while
drilling 12 ¼” section.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the activities that are to be carried out
immediately prior to and during the drilling of 12 ¼” hole section.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  Ensure  that the  drilling and associated operations are carried out  in
      accordance with the defined operations programme and are conducted in
     a safe and efficient manner.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of the Drilling and associated operations.
   •  Ensure that the unit has sufficient resources and supplies to perform
        drilling and associated activities as per contractual requirements.
   •  Confirm the calculations made by the driller.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.
   •  Measure and tally the next casing string.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure that accurate tallies and depth control is maintained.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s representative if a deviation
      from the planned operations occurs.

Assistant Driller:

   •  Maintain pipe tally, drilling tally and BHA records accurately and up to date.
   •  Supervise and assist with drill floor, pump/pit room, shaker house, mixing
     and fluid conditioning operations.
   •  Pre-plan and make ready the correct tools, equipment and necessary
     manpower for forthcoming operations.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                81


────────────────────────────────────────────

   •  Ensure that the Driller’s instructions to crewmembers are carried out.
   •  Bring to the attention of the Driller anything that may affect the safe and
     smooth running of the operation.
   •  Assist third party personnel involved in drilling operations.
   •  Check, measure, document and inspect the tubulars and drilling tools to be
      run in the hole.
   •  Assist with deck operations when required.

Subsea Engineer

   •  Pressure test all BOPs and associated well control equipment.
   •  Responsible for running and setting the wear bushing.

Deck Crew:

   •  Transfer of equipment to and from the drill floor in the correct sequence as
       required.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3.  Lifting gear inspected.
    4. PPE worn at all times.
    5. Weather conditions in the working range of the vessel.
    6. Yellow & Red Alarms tested.
    7. Well control equipment tested and lined up for drilling.
    8. Back up safety valves ready for use on rig floor.
    9.  Kill sheet updated.
   10. Kill mud available.
   11. Non shearable procedure posted in DCR.
   12. Drilling program available and instructions given in writing to the driller.
   13. Mud characteristics and mud volume ready as per program.
   14. Casing pressure tested.
   15. Nominal seat protector installed in the wellhead.
   16. All Bottom Hole Assembly components measured with  all dimensions
      recorded on drawings and ready for picking up. This includes drill bit and
       nozzles.

General:




                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                82


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 83 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.

                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations

    1. The minimum quantity of barite (or any alternative weighting material) held
     on board will be sufficient to raise the density of the mud system by at least
      1ppg, where:
             •  Mud system = hole volume (no pipe) + 150 BBLS surface
               volume.
    2. The minimum quantities of bulk cement and cement additives held on
      board will be sufficient to set at least two (2) 150M cement plugs in the
      hole section being drilled.
    3.  Drilling operations will be stopped if minimum stock levels are reached.
    4. Pressure test the BOPs and  all associated well control equipment as
      defined in the well control manual.
    5.  Install the 16¾ x 13 ⅜" nominal bore protector in the wellhead and rack the
       running/pulling tool in the derrick. CMC (Crown Mounted compensator) to
     be used when pulling full bore tools through the BOP and slip joint shoe.
    6. Lay out the 14 ¾” x 17 ½” bottom hole assembly.
    7. Make up and rack the 9⅝" casing hanger and pup joint assembly to the
       pack-off assembly, running tool and sub sea plug launching system with
      the Toolpusher, Operator Drilling Supervisor, Wellhead Service Engineer
     and Cementer.
    8. Make up and rack the cementing and dart-launching head to a stand of
        drifted landing string.
    9.  Verify that the float equipment is PDC drillable  if  it is intended to use a
    PDC bit.

Operation:

    1. Pick up and rack at least enough drill pipe to complete the next section.
    2. Make up and RIH the 12 ¼” bottom hole assembly (refer to procedures
       4.2.1.3 – Picking Up and Laying Out Tubulars above 61/2”OD, 4.2.1.7 –
      Tripping Drill Collars 8” OD and below, 4.2.1.8 – Tripping 91/2” Drill
       Collars). Test the MWD (measurement while drilling) or LWD (logging
       while drilling) tools and down hole motor if used.
    3. Run in the hole with drill pipe until the 12 ¼” Bit is approximately 1 stand
      above the predicted top of cement.
    4. Open the CMC (Crown Mounted Compensator) while the  bit passes
      through the BOP, space out so the bit is not in the BOPs while the slips are
       set.
    5. Inform DP when the BHA is passing through the BOP.
    6. Carry out kick drills and conduct a choke drill prior to drilling out the shoe
       track. Circulate up through the choke and have the  kill line open for
      pressure readings.
    7. Pick up the drilling stand  if used and with no rotation, break circulation,
      then slowly run in and tag the plug.
    8.  Drill out the cement plug(s), float collar, shoe track and float shoe.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                83


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 84 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir

9. Float equipment should be drilled at high flow rates with high WOB and low
  RPM and reamed several times to remove any cement or plug and float
   debris which may cause problems later.
10. Note and record the depth  of the shoe and inform Toolpusher and
   Operator drilling supervisor of any major discrepancy.
11. Clean out the 14 ¾ x 17 ½" rat hole as the program dictates.
12. Drill 3m of new hole, circulate and condition the mud and pull back into the
   shoe.
13. Where the BOPs are to be used for well control, as a minimum a
   formation  integrity  test should be conducted followed by slow
   circulation rates and choke line friction values.
14. Drill ahead and once the stabilizers are out of the shoe and in new hole
   then the rotary speed may be increased. Parameters will be at Operator
    Drilling Supervisor instruction.
15. Take any directional surveys as required by the programme.
16. The  riser may be boosted as  required  or as per Operator  Drilling
   Supervisor instruction.
17. Take slow circulating rates at the start of each shift and when the mud
   properties are changed.
18. Choke  line  friction values should be taken when the mud properties
   change.
19. Drill to the casing point plus the rat hole required, normally 5-6m.
20. Prepare the 9 ⅝” casing.
21. Inspect the nominal seat protector and pressure test the BOPs at intervals
   specified in the drilling programme and Well Control Manual.
22. At the casing point, circulate the hole clean and make a wiper trip to the
   casing shoe if required.
23. If bridges or excessive fill are encountered, then repeat the wiper trips until
   the hole is in an acceptable condition to run open hole electric logs or the
   casing (refer procedure 04 for tripping parameters).
24. Flow checks are to be performed before pulling out, when the bit is at the
   lowest casing shoe and prior to the BHA being pulled into the BOP. Extra
   care to be taken when the stabilizers and bit enter the shoe and remove
   the diverter packer before pulling the BHA.
25. It may be required to perform a SLM (steel line measure) while pulling out
    of the hole and report any difference in the drilled depth to the Toolpusher.
26. If open hole logs are carried out, then a wiper trip may be required before
   running the casing.
27. Circulate and condition the mud as per the cementing programme, ready
    to run the casing.
28. Wash the wellhead area when pulling out of the hole to run the casing.
    Verify that the bit is below the well head and not in the BOPs. As an
    alternative the BOP can be flushed through the choke and kill lines.
29. Retrieve and inspect the nominal seat protector. Details regarding any
   wear as well as wear orientation will be recorded in the IADC report. Do
   not allow the string to rotate while recovering the wear bushing.

   30. Lay out the wear bushing running retrieving tool.
   31. Prepare to run the 9 ⅝” casing.

Post Task Actions:

    2. Ensure all stabilisers are clean after racking back
    3. Rig up the wireline for logging , if required
    4. Prepare casing equipment as per procedure 4.1.13 – Running and

    5. Monitor well when out of hole

    7. Review the procedure and JSA for any improvement opportunities.

4.1.13 Running and Cementing 95/8” Casing

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
running and cementing the 9 ⅝” casing.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the running and cementing of the 9 ⅝” casing.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Senior Toolpusher / Jr Toolpusher:

   •  Supervise and control all activities of this process.
   •  To ensure that the operations are carried out in accordance with the
      defined operations programme and are conducted in a safe and efficient
      manner.
   •  Supervise operations in the moon pool.
   •  Confirm that any calculations made are correct.
   •  Consult with the O.I.M. and Operator’s Drilling Supervisor regarding the
      progress of operations.
   •  Keep the O.I.M. informed of all events and occurrences likely to cause or
      have caused a deviation from the operational schedule.

Subsea Engineer:

   •  Assist as required with moonpool activities.

Driller:

   •  Supervise and conduct all rig floor activities associated with this process.
   •  Ensure tallies and depth measurements are accurate.
   •  Inform the on tour Toolpusher and Operator’s  Drilling Supervisor  if a
       deviation from the planned operations occurs.

Crane Operator / Deck Crew:

   •  Supervise and conduct all deck operations associated with this process.
   •  Support all rig floor and moon pool activities as required.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                86

                         Issue Date

   •  Bring to the attention of the on tour Toolpusher any occurrence that may
       affect the safe and smooth efficient running of operations.

Procedure:

Safety Precautions:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis)..
    2. Permit to work fully approved and signed with recommendations.
    3.  Lifting gear fully inspected (check for appropriate colour coding, damage,
       malfunction, etc).
    4. PPE to be worn all the times. Chin straps to be worn on the drill floor.
    5.  All non essential personnel to be kept clear of Drill floor & Casing Bay.
    6. Ensure weather conditions are within the working range of the vessel.
    7. Yellow & Red Alarms to be tested.
    8. Ear protection to be worn in high noise areas.

Preparation Prior to Running Casing:

Consider the following items when preparing to run 9 5/8” casing:

    1. Read through and check Operator’s casing program.
    2. The casing shall be laid out on the pipe deck; a working bay should be
      kept beside the cat walk. An accurate count of all casing received will be
     made.
    3. Check that protectors are removed and that pin and boxes are cleaned.
    4.  All the Casing will be drifted, inspected, measured and an accurate tally
     made. The drift will conform to API Spec 5CT.
    5. Lengths and joint numbers will be painted on the casing if not done ashore.
      This to Include the pup joints but with letters (A, B, C etc).
    6. Any joints not to be run, rejected or which could not be drifted will be
       clearly marked and easily identifiable and recorded on the casing tally.
    7. The threads on shoe track joint,  will be thoroughly cleaned, dried and
       “black bagged”. Shoe track joints shall be checked for debris, prior to being
      picked up.
    8. Check that the collars on the shoe track joints, the float and the shoe are
         all thread-locked.
    9. Stop collars and centralizers can be fitted on deck, where possible.
   10. All lifting equipment will have current certification and colour coding. It will
     be inspected and made ready at the work site, with tag lines.
   11. Casing  handling equipment  to be prepared &  inspected  (side door
       elevator, single joint elevator, casing slip, rotary bushing inserts, casing fill
     up system etc). Test fit the elevators on all the grades of casing to be run.
   12. Single joint elevators will be connected to the v-door air winch on the rig
        floor and correct size confirmed by the asst driller.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                87


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 88 از 286 ]

                     Issue Date

13. Dog collar clamp adjusted to size and hand slips with rope for securing the
   handles.
14. Back-up casing tongs with long snub line set to casing size.
15. “Clamp on” air operated protectors shall be checked and made ready at
   the work site.  Dedicated airlines and attachments will be secured with
   whip checks.
16. A rope and hook will be hung on the v door ramp for returning the “Clamp
   ons” to the catwalk.
17. FMS power slips (if used) dressed with the correct size inserts, guides, will
   be lubricated, function tested and slung ready for picking up.
18. The Operator Drilling Supervisor will prepare a casing running list for the
    Driller.   This  will have been checked and approved by  the Senior
   Toolpusher. The joints will be marked where they enter the BOP and open
   hole. Distribute same to  driller, deck crew and crane operator when
   approved.
19. While POOH, strap the pipe (SLM (steel line measure)) if required by the
   Operator Drilling supervisor.  Result to be communicated to the Senior
   Tool pusher.
20. Wash and jet wash the wellhead area as per operational requirements.
21. Recover the wear bushing. To get an accurate measurement the Landing
   String should be used along with a Datum line, where possible.
22. Drift the landing string.
23. Clear the Drill Floor of any excess equipment not required for the casing
    job.
24. Stabbing Board to be checked, as per the check list, and lubricated. The
   Casing crew  shall be given  instructions  in  its use. To include  clear
    instructions on the hand signals required for the driller when the board flap
    is retracted.
25. A fall arrestor is to be installed above the Stabbing board prior to use.
26. Rig up to run casing using side door elevators.
27. If FMS (Flush Mounted Slips) are to be used they can be run from the
    start.
28. Casing dope, barite and thread cleaning fluid, pressure washer, thread
   locking compound and rags will be required.
29. Centralizers, stop collars, nails, hand tools and goggles if centralizers not
   previously installed.
30. Run test the power casing tong. Any time the power tong has to be laid
   down or not in use, the hydraulic power unit will be turned off and the
   pressure bled off. The tong will be functioned to verify status.
31. All handling gear to have current certification and be within limitations of
   job requirement.
32. All slings and shackles will have current certification, colour coding and
   inspected prior to use.  All shackles will be of 4 parts, ensure retaining
   rings/pins installed.
33. Record all pit volumes. Inform the logging unit of line up and operation.
34. Circulating Swedge, check same as casing thread.

                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                88


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 89 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and

   35. Ensure thread compatible stabbing valve is made up. All crew members
         will be instructed in its use and the location.
   36. Space out of the Landing string calculated to give a workable stick up
      above the rotary table.
   37. Remove the rotary inserts and Master bushings and install the FMS slips if
      they are to be used. Install hole cover.
   38. A Pre-job Safety meeting with all personnel involved will be held prior to
       rigging up. These will also be held at shift changes. If the job changes at
      any time, for any reason, a meeting should be held to discuss the way
       forward. Remind all crew members to look up at all times, and ensure the
      stabbing board operators are fully aware of signals needed for the driller to
      confirm the elevator status and to confirm that the flap is retracted.
   39. Establish communications with the Stabber and check on the signals to be
      used.
   40. Clean the Derrick camera as required.
   41. Review and discuss the procedures and JSA (Job Safety Analysis) for the
       job.
   42. The crews are to be made aware that casing will continually be brought
       into the drill floor and as such they should not work with their backs to the
    V door.
   43. All personnel working on the drill floor will wear chinstraps.
   44. Pressure test the cement hose and Lo-Torque valve assembly as per the
     cement program. This may be done off line.
   45. Plugs, darts installed in the cement head and plug launcher. This should
     be done  off the  critical path where  possible Personnel  involved  for
        installing plugs: Toolpusher, Operator Drilling Supervisor, Cementer and
      Wellhead Engineer.
   46. Cement Head made up and racked in the Derrick, one single up.
   47. The Hanger, running tool and sub sea launcher to be made up and racked
        in  the  Derrick.   If  required  the pack  off assembly can be  installed
      immediately prior to running.
   48. Dimensional and visual checks on the casing hanger and running tool.
   49. Prepare Mix water and Spacers as per the cement program. Be aware of
     dead volume. Check pit space for displacement. Possibly need a boat
       alongside.
   50. Check all lifting gear; ensure the rings are installed in the shackles.
   51. Install the side door elevators and rig up a tailing in rope.
   52. The safe and smooth running of a casing string depends upon safety
      awareness, good teamwork and excellent communication between  all
      personnel involved in the operation.

Running Casing:

    1. Prepare the shoe joint on the catwalk with crane and sling 1m above the
      shoe and drill floor air winch and sling 2m below the collar.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                89


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 90 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

2. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator, winch driver,
   and deck foreman hoist the shoe joint to the drill floor keeping  it slightly
   below horizontal to prevent  it swinging into the drill floor when  it reaches
   the top of the V door ramp.
3. Slowly bring the joint into the drill floor and land on the deck. Remove the
   protector and prepare the box as per the Operator Drilling Supervisor
    instructions for thread locking.
4.  Prior to lifting the shoe joint with the blocks install the #2 bushings inserts
   and install a hole cover.
5. Rotate the side door elevators to face the V door ramp and open them.
6. Pick up shoe joint with the drill floor winch and using the crane, guide the
    joint into the elevators. When in the correct position, drop the joint firmly
    into the elevators to turn them vertical causing them to close. Check they
   are properly latched and install the retaining pin.
7. Slack off and remove the drill floor winch and sling.
8. Ensuring good communication between the crane operator and deck
   foreman, hoist the shoe joint simultaneously slacking off on the catwalk
   winch and crane, keeping the float shoe clear of the deck.
9. When near vertical take the weight with the tailing rope, slack off the crane
   and remove the sling.
10. During Steps 11 through 16, the crane can place the next joints onto the
   pipe deck. All personnel to stay well clear of the joint as it is being moved
    into position and landed - tag lines must be used.
11. Swing the shoe in to well centre and run in hole. Set the slips and fill it with
   mud, then pick up far enough to observe the mud draining out of the shoe.
12. Run the joint back in and set the slips. Check the level of the mud, to see if
      it has dropped. Secure the slip handles with rope (first 5 joints) and install
   a single joint elevator or safety clamp above the slips.
13. If using flush mounted slips these should be used from the start and no
   dog collar is required. Only the Casing crew will operate the FMS (Flush
   Mounted Slips) on instructions from the man on the brake.
14. Unlatch the elevators.
15. Mark off on the casing tally all the joints as they are run. Record any
   deviation in the running order to the Toolpusher and the Client rep.
16. Pick up the next shoe track joint (intermediate) using steps 1 to 9, keeping
      it secure with the tailing in rope. Do not hold the casing with hands inside
   the pin or box connections.
17. When both joints ready clean the pin (be aware of steel splinters on the
   threads), and apply thread locking compound to the last third of the pin end
   thread.
18. Stab into the joint in the rotary table using the correct stabbing guide, slack
    off slightly on the elevators. The stabber will guide the casing from the
   stabbing board while it is spun in (unlatch the elevators if required). Do not
   hold the casing with hands inside the pin or box connections.



                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                90


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 91 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

19. Pick up or swing in the power tong, spin in and make the connections up to
   the required torque/ mark as per the operator instructions, using a back up
   tong and long snub line as required.
20. Remove the power and back up tongs and rack back or lower to the floor.
  Be aware of pinch points when using the tongs and use the correct
   handles.
21. When the joint has been torqued up, the Stabber will latch the side-door
   elevators and signal the Driller to confirm, and then signal again once the
   board extension is raised.
22. Remove the dog collar and pick up the weight, pull the slips and slowly run
    in the hole.
23. Record the pit volumes when starting and monitor the displacement while
   running casing.
24. Continue running the shoe track  joints applying locking compound as
   required (typically 3 joints).
25. Fill the casing with mud and pull back until the shoe is visible to check that
  mud passes through the float and shoe. RIH again and set the slips. Check
   the level of the mud; it should below the float collar.
26. Install the single joint elevators in a bridle below the side door elevators.
   Ensure that the safety pin is in position to prevent the elevators from
   unlatching.
27. Install a Clamp-on protector on the pin end of the next joint and using a
     drill floor air winch fitted with a set of single joint elevators, bring the next
    joint into the V- door ramp.
28. Set the joint onto the stepped ramp (if fitted) on the V door ramp. Slack off
   and remove the air winch. If the stepped ramp is not used then a sling will
   be used to lift and hold the joint until the pick up elevators on the top drive
   take the weight.
29. Remove the box end protector and dope the thread (this only to be done at
   the V door).
30. Latch the pick up elevators on the blocks onto the joint at the v door.
31. Pick up on the blocks and using tailing rope to control the joint, bring it into
   the floor. Keep it held back from the joint in the rotary table. Do not allow
   anyone to walk between the suspended joint and the one in the rotary or to
   walk under the joint as it is being picked up.
32. Remove the “Clamp-on” protectors with feet well out of the way and lower
   them down the V door, do not throw them. Stab into the joint, in the Rotary
    table, remove the stabbing guide.
33. Slack off on the single joint elevators and clamp the casing tong onto the
    joint.
34. The man on the stabbing board will unlatch the pick ups and signal to
   lower the elevators depending type used.
35. The Stabber will rock the pipe to assist in getting the threads started. Once
   spun in the elevators will lowered over the box until the elevators are below
   the box collar. Once torqued up, latch the elevators install the retaining pin.


                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                91


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 92 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date

   36. Remove and secure the tongs. The Stabber will confirm that the elevators
      are latched and the stabbing board has been raised.
   37. Pick up and take the string weight pull the slips and RIH at controlled
      speed. Monitor the displacement. Check for losses or gains with the mud
       loggers.
   38. While making up the joints, the Crane crew will be preparing the catwalk
       with more casing. A joint can be pulled into the V-door.
   39. Set the  slips  (tying the handles and  installing the dog  collar/pick up
       elevators as required) latch the pick up elevators on the joint in the V- door
      ensuring the retaining pin is installed correctly.
   40. When there is sufficient weight in the string the back up tong will not be
       required.
   41. Continue running the 9⅝” casing as per the tally  filling every joint and
      completely filling  it every 5 joints. 'Listen' for the height of the mud in the
      casing from a distance not over the top of the joint.
   42. Monitor the string weight and  if the weight reaches 80% of the elevators
    SWL (safe working load) then change to the 500ton elevators and spiders.
   43. Check all the lifting gear on a regular basis.
   44. Confirm that the correct number of joints has been run and the correct
     number remain on deck.
   45. Continue to run casing as the above procedure, until the hanger is to be
      picked up. Pick up and make up the casing hanger assembly to the last
        joint of casing. Land the casing hanger in the rotary (record the total weight
      before landing).
   46. Release the running tool and install the pack off  if not already installed.
     Make up the running tool again. Check the running tool as required by the
      Service Engineer. Toolpusher and Operator Drilling Supervisor must be on
      the drill floor at this time.
   47. Ensure the casing is full prior to running drill pipe.

Post Task Actions:

    1.  Prior to picking up the hanger and landing string, count the number of
        joints left on the deck. Verify the correct number have been run.
    2. Change over to drill pipe handling equipment.
    3. Clear the drill floor of any equipment no longer required.
    4. Clean up the drill floor.

Run Casing on Landing String:

    1. Run the casing on the landing string.  Fill up the string on each stand.
    2. To reduce surge pressures, open the kill and choke lines to surface while
      running in with the hanger.
    3. Open  the CMC (Crown Mounted Compensator) when  the casing  is
       entering the BOP.
    4. Inform DP when casing entering BOP.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                92


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 93 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    5. Run the casing at controlled speed to prevent surge pressures. RIH at
      slow speed in open hole.
    6.  Prior to going into open hole, make up the top drive and break circulation.
      Record up and down weights.
    7. Work the pipe slowly through any ledges or tight spots and limit the set
     down weight. Inform Toolpusher and Operator Drilling Supervisor.
    8.  If unable to RIH make up the top drive and circulate down as required with
      low strokes. The Toolpusher must be on the drill floor.
    9. Monitor well closely for losses/gains.
   10. Before the casing hanger enters the BOP pick up the cementing and dart
      launching head and space out to have the cementing head within 10m of
      the drill floor while cementing. Install a lo-torque valve and cementing hose
      secured with safety wires as required.
   11. Verify the lo-torque valve and the cement head circulating valves are open.
   12. Open and adjust the CMC as required.
   13. Using the mud pumps and circulating through the cement manifold start
       circulating and continue to run in, and land the hanger in the wellhead
      housing.
   14. Verify the hanger has landed at the correct depth.
   15. Break circulation slowly with the rig pumps using drilling mud. Circulate at
       least the volume of the casing and landing string.
   16. Limit the pump rate so that the annular velocity is no greater than that used
       to drill the 12 ¼” hole.
   17. Observe for mud losses while circulating.

Post Task Actions:

    1. During the  circulation, inform the Marine Department  to  fluff up and
       pressurize the Cement silos.
    2. Service the mud pump fluid ends and change piston and liner sizes as
       required
    3. Clean high and low pressure mud pump strainers.
    4. Ensure the cementer is ready.
    5. Mud balance and cups in cement room.
    6. Ensure surge tank line is clear.
    7. Communication system between cement unit and driller to be tested.
    8. Mud pits and lines cleaned and flushed ready for mixing spacers or cement
      mix water.
    9. Transfer lines from mud pits to cement unit cleaned and flushed.
   10. Mix the cement mix water.
   11. Mix the spacer when requested (ensure no loss or contamination).
   12. Record pit volumes.

Cementing 9 5/8” Casing:

Preparations prior to cement job:

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures                93

     1. Double check calculations with cementing engineer and Operator Drilling
        Supervisor.
     2. Advise DP room and engine room that the job is about to start. Double
       check weather forecast.
     3. Ensure mechanic and electrician are standing by in case of emergency.
     4. The following actions to be taken by the Derrick Man  / Mud Supervisor
      and double checked by the Assistant Driller:
           •  Cement lines flushed and dried.
           •  Bulk air system fully operational.
           •  Ensure  rig floor cement manifold and cement lines have been
              flushed and ready for use.
     5. Mix water ready.
     6. Spacer lined up to cement unit.




Cement Job:

    1. Flush and pressure test the lo torque valve, cementing hose and surface
       lines to 5000psi (or as per the cementing programme).
    2. Line up the spacer and transfer to the cement unit as required.
    3. Pump the spacer as cementing program.
    4. Line up to transfer the mix water to the cement unit and also to circulate on
         itself to prevent deadheading of the pump.
    5. Mix and pump cement slurry as per the program. Take weights and
      samples as required.
    6. Monitor  for  lost  circulation throughout the job compare with the mud
      logging unit and report any major discrepancies.
    7. Release the top plug launching dart, displace the dart with the cement unit
       to the top subsea plug. Shear out the plug.
    8. Change over to the mud pumps and displace the top plug to the float collar
      using drilling mud.
    9. Do not over displace the cement by more than 50% of the shoe track
      volume.
   10. When the plug has bumped, pressure up the casing to the required test
      pressure as per the cementing program. Hold the pressure  for the
       specified time, record the volume required to pressure up the casing.
   11. Release the pressure watching for returns.
   12. If the float is not holding, then pump the volume bled back and hold the
       shut-in pressure until the cement takes its initial set.
   13. Rig down the cement hose and lo-torque valve.
   14. Set the casing hanger seal assembly as per the wellhead service engineer
       instructions.

                         Issue Date

   15. Flush through the choke and kill lines and circulate the riser contents.
   16. Close a set of pipe rams and pressure test the seal assembly as required
      by the drilling program.
   17. Verify that all surface lines have been flushed and are cement free.
   18. Disengage the wellhead running tool from the wellhead using the CMC
   19. Pull out, inform DP when clear of BOP. Use CMC (Crown Mounted
      Compensator) when pulling full bore tools through the BOP and slip joint
      shoe.
   20. Lay down the running tool.
   21. Strap  the  landing  string (SLM) on  the  trip  out and  calculate  the
     measurement from the rotary table to the top of casing hanger if required.
   22. Verify that the ship is at normal drilling draft.
   23. Apply tide corrections and report this depth in the IADC report.



Post Task Actions:

    1. Flush the cement lines clear of slurry.
    2. Purge the bulk transfer lines to the surge tank.
    3. Vent off the bulk tanks.
    4. Check the remaining stock of cement.
    5. Ensure that the surge tank is empty and made ready for the next job.
    6. Clean out the tanks used for spacer and mix water.
    7. Flush the mud mix lines.

Cement calculations:

    1. Cement required (MT) = Slurry required (ltrs) / Yield (ltrs/MT).
    2. Mix Water required (M3) = Cement required (MT) x Mix Water ratio (M3/
      MT).

Displacement:

    1. Volume of Landing String: Capacity of DP (ltrs/m) x Length of String (m) +
      Capacity of Stinger (ltrs/m) x Length of Stinger (m).
    2. Displacement: Volume String + Volume Stinger + safety margin.
