4.2.5 Manriding


 4.2.5.1 Manrinding Operations

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to explain the process to be followed when lifting
personnel with air/hydraulic hoists.

Scope:

The procedure describes the entire process including the safety precautions to be
exercised and includes the equipment to be used when manriding.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Tool Pusher/ Driller/ Assistant Driller

Attachments:

Man Riding Check list.

Manriding Equipment:

If there is no alternative to manriding the following equipment should be used with
the listed safety features.

Winch:

   •  Limited maximum  pull, (counter balanced system  for use on  floating
        installations).
   •  Independent secondary braking system.
   •  Slack line shut-off mechanism.
   •  Control lever returns to neutral (STOP), push lever away = pay out
     (LOWER).
   •  Pull lever = heave in (RAISE).
   •  Crown Block Sheave designed for manriding. (Secondary retention for the
      whole assembly including sheave).
   •  Emergency stop facility (unobstructed).
   •  Wire rope termination to comply with current standards.
   •  Spooling device.
   •  Drum guard.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               184


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 185 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   •  Upper and lower travel limit switches.
   •  Emergency lowering facility.

Harness:

   •   Full body type.
   •   'D' rings for carrying hand tools.
   •  Rescue or fall arrest lanyard (see 5).
   •  Safe system for tools aloft (lanyards etc).

PPE:

   •  In addition to normal rig PPE, wear a high visibility jacket or manrider
      armbands.
   •  Always fit and use a chin strap on the hard hat.

Connecting Devices:

   •  Must incorporate a simple locking device which can only be manually
       activated.
   •  Overhoist protection placed 1m above manrider, e.g. oversized end link.

Secondary Fall Arrest Equipment:

   •  Use an additional safety device (where practical) attached to a safe part of
      the harness (fall arrestor, inertia reel, line locker, pennant line).

Radios:

    •  Ensure there is a system to maintain and issue radios.
    •  When radios are appropriate for the job, establish a Radio Communication Voice
       Procedure:
    •  Use a dedicated channel where possible.
    •  Always speak slowly and clearly, never talk over or interrupt.
    •  At the end of each communication say "over", only then is it safe for someone
       else to speak.
    •  Never use the radio to have a chat with your mates.
    •  Assign everyone a call sign and use it (winch control, manrider, driller etc).
    •  Use continuous direction commands i.e., (up, up, up, up, up, / down, down, down,
      down/ stop, stop, stop).
    •  Remember NO COMMAND = STOP THE AIR WINCH.
    •    If there are long periods of silence, call for a radio check with everyone involved.
    •    If your radio stops working, contact the team by another means ASAP and get it
       replaced. If you are the manrider use your hand signals to return to the rig floor.

Procedure:

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               185


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 186 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by


Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1.  Lifting and lowering personnel by air/hydraulic hoist can be hazardous
      unless extreme caution  is exercised and should be avoided whenever
       possible and can be undertaken only when an essential task must be done
     and when there is absolutely no practical and safe alternative to manriding
       to accomplish the task.
    2. Manriding operations shall be controlled by the Permit to Work procedure
      as Company Policy: The manriding checklist will be used as part of this
       Policy.
    3. A checklist shall be completed every time manriding is undertaken.
    4. The provisions within the checklist shall be complied with every time
      manriding is undertaken.
    5. Copies of completed manriding checklists shall be retained in the rig’s files
      along with the permit to work records for audit purposes.

Man riding Requirements

    1. When a hoist is used for manriding, no other equipment should be lifted
       with the manriding hoist at the same time, i.e. lifting a chicksan along with
     an employee on the hoist.
    2. When using a hoist for manriding there are to be no concurrent operations
        in the area of the manriding operation, which can in any way interfere with
      the manriding activity.
    3. At no time should the blocks be moved nor pipe rotated when using a hoist
        for manriding. All moving equipment should be locked off.
    4. When a lifting basket is used it must be verified that the slings and basket
      have a current lifting certificate and colour coding. The basket should be
        fitted with a double pennant.
    5.  Certificate of Inspection and that the lifting operation and basket meet all
       local regulatory requirements.
    6. The  total weight  of  the  basket, equipment and  personnel must be
      determined to ensure the safe working load of the hoist and the basket
       slings are not exceeded.
    7. Personnel riding in the basket must have a safety line secured to the hoist
        line.
    8. When any equipment or tools are hoisted into the derrick, the area below
       shall be off limits to personnel, barriers and signs put in place and a PA
     announcement made.
    9. The counterbalancing effect of the weight of the hoist wire must be taken
       into consideration; the point at which the weight of the wire will exceed the
      weight of the person in the riding belt should be established and any
      manriding operations close to or above this height should be subject to
       additional controls.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               186


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 187 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   10. Consideration should also be given to contingency planning, i.e. rescue of
      personnel in the event of power loss to hoist.
   11. If for any reason the above provisions cannot be fully met, the necessity for
      conducting a non-conforming manrider operation shall be discussed with
      the Rig Manager.  If  it  is agreed   it  is necessary  to proceed, special
     measures  shall be put  into place to ensure equivalent safety of the
       operation.

Manriding Hoist:

    1. A minimum of one air/hydraulic hoist on the rig floor is to be certified for
      manriding and shall only be used for manriding operations.
    2. The air/hydraulic hoist shall meet the following specifications and safety
       features in its design:
          •  The hoist-operating lever shall automatically return to neutral on
             release from any operating position.
          •  An automatic brake shall be fitted so that it will apply whenever the
             operating lever is returned to neutral, or on loss of power.
          •  Provision of a secondary brake. This may be manually operated.
          •  A clutch capable of disengaging the drum will not be fitted.
          •    It shall be fitted with upper and lower limit switches, a slack wire
             detector device and overload protection to  limit pulling force to
           150kg (330lb).
          •  The hoist shall be fitted with an emergency stop device.
          •  Specific  written  instructions must be  available and followed  to
              protect personnel when man riding.
          •  A plate welded to the frame of the manriding air/hydraulic hoist
              stating "Manriding Hoist" is required.
          •  The air/oil supply to the hoist will be regulated to the manufacturers
          recommended pressure.

Procedure for Using a Manriding Air/Hydraulic Hoist:

    1. Only "manriding hoists", as defined in the previous section, shall be used
        for hoisting personnel.
    2. Safety hooks will not be attached to the hoist line when used for manriding.
    3. The riding belt must be shackled directly onto the end of the hoist line
       without any intervening chains, slings, swivels or other fittings.
    4. 4 part bow shackles with a factory made retaining pin or ring must be used
       to comply with Aban Drilling policy.
    5. The riding belt used will be of an approved standard, and inspected for
      wear and damage before use.
    6. Any tools carried into the derrick must be tied off to either the employee or
      the hoist tugger line (Only use tools from the “Tools Aloft Kit”).



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               187


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 188 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    7. The hoist operator shall be experienced, an authorized company hoist
      operator and judged competent by the supervisor.

Communication:

    1.  Prior to the job beginning, consideration will be given to communication
       with the employee on the hoist line and  all personnel involved in the
      operation will be briefed on the communication method to be used and
     emergency procedures related to the operation.
    2.  If the  hoist operator cannot  directly see the manrider, a competent
      signalman, who is in sight of and within clear voice range of the hoist
       operator, and with a clear view of the person in the riding belt, must be
      used to direct the hoist operator.
    3.  All visual signals and audible communications shall be continuous, e.g.
      “Up-Up-Up” or “Down-Down-Down” or “Stop-Stop-Stop”.
    4. Should  signals  or  communications  cease,  the  hoist  operator  shall
      immediately  stop movement  of  the  manrider and  shall  not resume
     movement until clearly directed to do so.
    5.  If the operation precludes the use of visual and audible signals directly
      from a signalman to the hoist operator, use of radio communications is
      acceptable.  All necessary steps  shall be taken  to ensure  the  radio
      communication is not interfered with by other radio usage.
    6.  In conformance with the provisions above, the communication shall be
      continuous.
    7. Where  radio  communications  are  the  primary  method  of  audible
      communication a method of supplemental visual signals must be in place
      as an  alternative method  of  communicating an  immediate “STOP”
     command, should radio communications be interrupted.
    8. The assigned personnel will complete the task and all personnel will carry
      out their designated duties without handing over to other individuals unless
       directed to do so by the on-duty Toolpusher.



Emergency Plans & Equipment:

Before any  manriding  operation can commence, a  rescue  plan must be
considered as part of the pre-task risk assessment and all relevant equipment
must be readily available.

It must be recognized that a rescue operation can introduce its own hazards,
therefore the planning and execution of this operation requires particular care,
consideration and further risk assessment before commencing.

The maximum recommended suspension time for any individual, in a harness, is
twenty minutes.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               188


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 189 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by


Any longer than this and the individual may start to lose circulation in the limbs
held by the harness,  this can  result  in discomfort and  at worse,  loss  of
consciousness. However, consideration must be given to repairing malfunctioning
equipment before attempting a rescue if the equipment can be repaired safely. If
the manrider is not in any immediate danger i.e., if the air supply to the winch fails
but can be reinstated within a short timeframe he may then be brought to safety
once the equipment is repaired.

Equipment that may be an integral part of the rig's Rescue Plan:

   •  Harness - Full body type fitted with a D ring suitable for rescue purposes.
   •  Alternative nitrogen or air supply to winch.
   •  Secondary winch.
   •  Rescue basket (for use with alternative winch).
   •  Cherry picker.
   •  Fully charged radios.
   •  Emergency manual lowering device on main manriding winch.
   •  Emergency Descent/Ascent device (personnel must be trained in use).

The use of emergency ascent/descent devices, such as Spanset and Gotcha,
should only be used if no other alternative can be found. Only trained personnel
should operate the devices.

As with any manriding task, during a rescue operation it is essential that,
should  circumstances  change, a  full reassessment  of  conditions  is
conducted to ensure the continued safety of everyone involved.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clean and Check harness for any damage and store in dedicated area.
    2. Check manrider  hoist  is spooled on drum  correctly and the wire  is
     undamaged.
    3. Secure wire to dedicated padeye and take up the slack.
    4. Ensure the winch wire is not fouled upon anything  i.e. travelling block,
       finger board etc.
    5. Remove any “do not use” signs from the drawworks, top drive, rotary
       controls etc.
    6. Unlock any equipment that was locked out.
    7. Before leaving ensure the control leaver  is in neutral, and turn off air
       supply.
    8. Sign off any permits that were taken out for the manriding operation.
    9. Review the procedure and the JSA.

 4.2.5.2 Working Overside (Including Moonpool)

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of working overside.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during working overside.

Responsible Persons:

Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Drill Crew

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Use only the designated Man Riding Winches.
    3. Ensure that the Man Riding Winches are clearly marked.
    4. Raise a PTW (Permit to Work) to meet the requirements for working over
      the side - the Standby Boat is to be in place.
    5. Ensure a full body harness is used.
    6. Check the work vest (life jacket) is in good condition.
    7. Check the harness for defects.
    8. The winch operator and the man carrying out the work are to discuss and
      agree the proper use of hand signals.
    9. The winch operator will remain at the winch at all times.
   10. Ensure that the winch operator knows fully the operation of the winch.
   11. Check the winch functions are operating correctly before use.
   12. During man riding operations the winch operator or deck foreman will keep
      the man aloft in sight at all times.
   13. If communications are lost between winch operator and the man in the
       riding harness, then introduce a third man to regain good communications.
           If this can not be achieved, the job will be suspended until  it is safe to
       continue.
   14. Check out the condition of the wire and its spooling on the drum.
   15. The Man Riding check list is to be completed prior to starting operation.
   16. Ensure good communications with the control room and stand by boat

Operation:

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               193


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 194 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011

Note: For further information on using the manriding winches, refer to procedure
4.2.5.1 – Manriding Operations.

    1. Don the harness ensuring all the buckles are secure. Shackle the winch
       wire to the D-ring on the harness ensuring the pin retainer is secure or use
     an approved carabineer.
    2. Don a work-vest (life jacket).
    3. The winch operator will operate the winch in accordance with the signals
      given by the man in the riding harness.
    4. Lower or raise the crewmember to and from the work site as required.
     Check the spooling of the wire on the drum while hoisting.
    5. Complete the job task.
    6. Unshackle the winch wire from the harness.
    7. Remove the work-vest and remove the harness.
    8. Upon completion of the work the winch operator to secure the area.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Stow away the harness by hanging it up from the rear D-ring in the Safety
      Equipment Locker.
    2. Secure the man riding winch live end to the designated secure point. Pick
     up the slack in the wire, ensuring it is correctly spooled onto the drum.
    3. Leave the work area in a safe working condition for the next job.
    4.  If appropriate stand down the standby boat.
    5. Sign off the Permit to Work.

4.2.6 Mud Pumps

 4.2.6.1 Changing Valves and Seats

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of changing the mud
pump valves and seats.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing the mud pump valves and seats.

Responsible Persons:

Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman/ Electrician

Procedure:

The mud pumps have two different types of valve caps fitted on them. Mud pump
1 and the riser booster pump have a National wedgelock valve cap; whereas mud
pumps 2 and 3 have a “Fast Fit” valve cap. Therefore there are two different
procedures for the different types of valve caps.

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedures and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW if required.
    3. Shut down the pumps.
    4. Close the stand pipe. Open the bleed off line and pump through to confirm
      the line is not plugged. Close the suction valves and open the suction
      bleed line.
    5. Continue circulating with the remaining pumps.
    6.  Isolate the pump electrically and mechanically.
    7. Tag or lock off all valves and switches.
    8. While the pump is being isolated, gather tools and equipment required.
       •  Equipment for pulling valves: Seat puller complete with hydraulic pump,
          Driver for the valve seat, flogging bar, sledge hammer, soapy water
        and brush.
    9. Inspect all hydraulic hoses, pumps and fittings prior to use.
   10. Refer to electrical isolation procedure.
   11. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               195


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 196 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   12. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   13. If any lifting gear is required for the task, check it is fit for purpose, colour
      coded and the certification is valid.
   14. Place a “Do not operate: isolated equipment” sign on mud pump controls in
      the DCR (Driller’s control room).

Operation:

National wedgelock valve caps:

    1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
        is zero pressure on the pump.
    2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
    3. Loosen the nut on the cover locking screw by at least 4 turns.
    4. Free the locking screw by tapping it down.
    5. Loosen the valve cover.
    6. Loosen the locking wedges by tapping down on the top of the wedges.
    7. Remove the cover.
    8.  If it is required to remove the wedges, remove the locking wedge screw.
    9. Remove the valve cover plug using the specific wrench provided.
   10. If required, remove the valve cover packing.
   11. The upper valve stem guide or insert may now be replaced  if necessary.
        Install a new valve stem guide insert when the old one is worn enough to
      permit 1/8” clearance between the valve stem and guide, by holding the
       insert with large pliers centred on the holder and driving it in with a sledge
     hammer.
   12. Remove the valve and valve spring.
   13. Insert the seat puller into the valve seat. Make up the thread until hand
        tight. Place the hydraulic jack over the puller bar and screw down the top
       nut, until fully seated. Ensure it is properly lined up.
   14. Connect the hydraulic jack pump to the hydraulic jack. Inspect all fittings
     on the pump, check the hose for any damage and ensure the jack is in the
       retracted position.
   15. Chain down the jack to prevent  it or the seat from jumping out of the
      module.
   16. Barrier off the immediate area.
   17. Place the seat into the module and tap lightly using the seat driver. Check
      the seat is sitting level in the seat. Re-install the driver and strike firmly
       three times. Pull the driver and check the seat is properly seated in the
      module. Run a finger under the seat and check gap. This should be approx
     ¼” or less.
   18. Pump the jack until the seat is pulled free.
   19. Dismantle the puller and lift out the seat.
   20. Clean  the  valve  seating  area  thoroughly,  inspect and ensure   it  is
      thoroughly dry. Check for wash outs.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               196


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 197 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   21. Install the seat into the module. Use the valve seat driver provided with the
     pump to drive the valve seat to obtain an initial seal.
   22. Install a new valve into the seat and centre the valve spring on the valve.
   23. Securely tighten the upper valve stem guide holder to the valve cover plug.
   24. Grease the valve cover packing and place  it in the counter-bore in the
       cylinder. Remove all debris before pushing the packing in place. Replace
      the packing if it is cut or broken at the outer edges.
   25. Coat the bottom seating surface, outside of the valve cover and the top
      tapered surface with “Kopr-Kote”.
   26. Set the valve cover assembly onto the wedges push down on the cover
     and hand tighten the nut on the cover locking screw. Ensure the bottom of
      the wedge adjusting screw is not drawn up inside the wedges.
   27. Thoroughly coat the cover screw threads and the threads in the threaded
       ring with “Kopr-Kote”.
   28. Install the assembly in the threaded ring and hammer up the cover until
        tight.
   29. Use a o.6m bar through both sides of the cover screw and hammer tight
       with 3 or 4 solid blows of a sledge hammer.
   30. Tighten the nut on the adjusting screw with the pull of one man on the
     1.75m extension wrench provided with the pump.
   31. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   32. Remove all mechanical and electrical isolations.
   33. Prime the pump and close the bleed off valve.
   34. Inform the driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps when ready to
       reactivate the pump.
   35. Line up the standpipe manifold.
   36. Remove the “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign from the mud pump
       control in the DCR.
   37. Continue circulating with the pumps as required.

Fast Fit valve Cap:

    1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
        is zero pressure on the pump.
    2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
    3. Use the flogging bar to loosen the valve cap. Unscrew and remove the
      valve cap and inspect the threads. Watch for pinch points.
    4.  Pull the valve retainer by hand.  It may be necessary to “knock” this to
      break the seal. Clean and inspect the sealing area and valve stem guide.
    5. Remove the spring and valve and clean them with soapy water and inspect
        for any damage.
    6. Remove any mud or fluid. Clean out inside of the module including the
     weep hole.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               197


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 198 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    7.  Insert the seat puller into the valve seat. Make up the thread until hand
        tight. Place the hydraulic jack over the puller bar and screw down the top
       nut, until fully seated. Ensure it is properly lined up.
    8. Connect the hydraulic jack pump to the hydraulic jack. Inspect all fittings
     on the pump, check the hose for any damage and ensure the jack is in the
       retracted position.
    9. Chain down the jack to prevent  it or the seat from jumping out of the
      module.
   10. Barrier off the immediate area.
   11. Under no circumstances should the hose or fittings be held in the hand
     when using the hydraulic pump, the hose must be of sufficient length to
      keep operator at a safe distance from the mud pump.
   12. Pump the jack until the seat is pulled free.
   13. Dismantle the puller and lift out the seat.
   14. Clean  the  valve  seating  area  thoroughly,  inspect and ensure   it  is
      thoroughly dry. Check for wash outs.
   15. Clean and dry the new valve seat.
   16. Place the seat into the module and tap lightly using the seat driver. Check
      the seat is sitting level in the seat. Re-install the driver and strike firmly
       three times. Pull the driver and check the seat is properly seated in the
      module. Run a finger under the seat and check gap. This should be approx
     ¼” or less.
   17. Install a new valve and the spring.
   18. Check the seal area in the module and on the valve retainer. Install a new
      valve retainer seal and insert the valve retainer.
   19. Clean and grease the threads on the valve cap, screw into the threaded
        ring. Tighten using the flogging bar. Do not over-tighten, this should be
     hand tight.
   20. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   21. Sign off the PTW.
   22. Electrically de-isolate the pump.
   23. Remove all mechanical and electrical isolations.
   24. Prime the pump and close the bleed off valve.
   25. Pressure up the pump to seat the new valve seat.
   26. Bleed off the pressure.
   27. Inform the driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps when ready to
       reactivate the pump.
   28. Line up the standpipe manifold.
   29. Remove the “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign from the mud pump
       control in the DCR.
   30. Continue circulating with the pumps as required.

Post Task Actions:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               198


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 199 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir

1. With the mud pump running, check  all parts are functioning correctly,
   check for leaks through the weep holes and seals etc.
2. Once the pump has been running, at first opportunity check tightness of
   valve cap.
3. Clean and return all tools to proper storage area.
4. Dispose of the old seat and valve.
5. Prepare a new seat and valve.
6. Inform the storekeeper which parts have been used.
7. Update the pump books.
8. Review the procedure and JSA.

 4.2.6.2 Changing a Piston

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of changing the mud
pump pistons.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing the mud pump pistons.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman/ Electrician

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW.
    3. Shut down the mud pumps.
    4. Close the stand pipe and suction valves. Open the bleed off line, pump
      through to confirm the line is not plugged, close the suctions valves and
     open the suction bleed line.
    5. Continue circulating with the remaining pumps.
    6.  Isolate the pump electrically and mechanically.
    7. While pump  is being  isolated, gather  tools and equipment  required.
      Equipment for piston and liner:
       •  Hammer, 1  3/8” Box  Spanner, Torque Wrench, 1  3/8”  Socket,
          Screwdriver, Pump Rotating Bar and Rest, Rags, Kopr-cote and
         Grease.
    8. Inspect all hydraulic hoses, pumps and fittings prior to use.
    9. Refer to electrical isolation procedure.
   10. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.
   11. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   12. If any lifting gear is required for the task, check it is fit for purpose, colour
      coded and the certification is valid.
   13. Place a “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign on the mud pump
       controls in the DCR (drillers control room).


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               200


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 201 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

Operation:

    1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically & electrically isolated and there is
      zero pressure on the pump.
    2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
    3. Clean down the piston rod chamber before removing the liner cooling
      shroud and hose.
    4. Remove the side plate and manually crank the pump until the piston is
      almost fully retracted. Loosen and remove the intermediate rod and piston
      rod clamps.
    5. Continue cranking the pump until there is sufficient clearance to remove
      the intermediate rod.
    6. Continue cranking the pump to extend the crosshead extension rod onto
      the piston rod. Loosely install the piston rod clamp and continue cranking
      the pump to withdraw the piston.
    7. Clean out the liner.
    8. Inspect the liner, liner seal and wear plate. Replace as required.
    9. Take the new previously prepared piston and apply grease to the rubber.
     Check it is the right size for the liner in use.
   10. Inspect the ends of the crosshead extension rod, intermediate rod and
       piston rod to check the condition of the alignment spigots. If worn, change
     them out.
   11. Line up the piston with the liner and crank the pump to push the piston into
      the liner with the cross head extension rod.
   12. Once the piston has been inserted into the liner to its full extent, continue
      cranking the pump to allow the intermediate rod to be inserted.
   13. Line up the intermediate rod to the crosshead extension rod and crank the
     pump until the intermediate is snug against the piston rod.
   14. Clean and inspect the piston rod clamps for cracking and coat the inside of
      the clamps with grease.
   15. Install the intermediate rod and piston rod clamps, and torque the bolts to
      (12-P-160:150 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 75 ft-lbs).
   16. Clean out the liner wash tank and refill with clean drill water.
   17. Install the cooling water shroud and hose.
   18. Remove the crank handle and re-install the side plate.
   19. Replace the piston rod chamber covers.
   20. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   21. Sign off the PTW.
   22. Electrically de-isolate the pump.
   23. Remove all mechanical isolations.
   24. Prime the pump and close bleed-off valve.
   25. Inform the Driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps.
   26. Line up the stand pipe manifold.
   27. Remove the “Do no operate, equipment isolated” signs from the DCR
        (drillers control room).

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               201


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 202 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   28. Continue circulating with the pumps, as required.

Post Task Actions:

    1. With the mud pump running check  all parts are functioning correctly.
       Inspect for any leaks through weep holes, seals etc.
    2. Ensure the liner wash and lube oil pumps are running.
    3. Clean and tidy away all tools. Bleed of air and coil away airlines.
    4. Dispose of the old piston.
    5. Inform the storekeeper which parts have been used.
    6. Prepare a new piston.
    7. Update the pump books.
    8. Clean and inspect any lifting gear used for damage. Return equipment
      used to the rigging loft, reporting any damage.
    9. Review the procedure and JSA.

 4.2.6.3 Changing a Liner

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of changing the mud
pump liners.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing the mud pump liners.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman / Electrician

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW.
    3. Shut down the mud pumps.
    4. Close the stand pipe valves. Open the bleed off valve and pump through to
      confirm the line is not plugged. Close the suction valves and open the
       suction bleed line.
    5. Continue circulating with the remaining pumps.
    6.  Isolate the pump electrically and mechanically.
    7. While the pump is being isolated, gather all tools and equipment required.
      Equipment required for changing a piston and liner:
       •  Hammer, 2  3/16” box spanner, ¾” Ratchet Drive and 2  3/16” socket,
          screwdriver, pump rotating bar and rest, rags, Kopr-cote and grease.
    8. Refer to electrical isolation procedure.
    9. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.
   10. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   11. Place a “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign on mud pump controls in
      the DCR (drillers control room).
   12. If any lifting gear is required, check it is fit for purpose, colour coded and in
        certification.

Operation:


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               203


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 204 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
    is zero pressure on the pump.
2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
3. Clean down the piston rod chamber before removing the liner cooling
   shroud and hose.
4. Remove the side plate and manually crank the pump until the piston is
   almost fully retracted. Loosen and remove the intermediate rod and piston
   rod clamps.
5. Continue cranking the pump until there is sufficient clearance to remove
   the intermediate rod.
6. Continue cranking the pump to extend the crosshead extension rod onto
   the piston rod. Loosely install the piston rod clamp and continue cranking
   the pump to withdraw the piston.
7. Allow the mud to drain from the liner and wash out the piston rod chamber
8. Loosen and remove the liner clamp bolts. Re-insert the bolts, one through
   each side of the clamp as handles. Tap the clamp to remove it.
9.  Insert the wedge tool between the pilot bushing and the liner to loosen the
    liner. Choke a small canvas or steel sling (1/2 ton) around the liner and use
   a chain block and the trolley beam on the front of the pump to remove the
    liner and hoist it out of the piston rod chamber.
10. Clean and inspect the wear plate and pilot bushing for any damage. Use
   emery paper to remove any traces of the old liner seal and clean the
   locating rings.
11. Ensure the new liner  is clean and paint has been removed from the
   locating rings.
12. Lift the liner onto the pump using the chain hoist and trolley beam on the
    front of the pump.
13. Lubricate the locating rings on the liner with copper coat and insert it into
    retaining ring. Check the seal and make sure  it is still located in the liner
     lip.
14. Clean and inspect the liner clamp and coat the inside of the clamp with
   grease.
15. Slide the liner into the pilot bushing and push firmly home (check the liner
   seal remains in position).
16. Install the liner clamp and tighten the bolts. Torque them up to the required
   torque (12-P-160: 250 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 100 ft-lbs).
17. Take the new, previously prepared piston and apply grease to the rubber.
   Check the piston is the correct size for the liner in use.
18. Inspect the ends of the crosshead extension rod, intermediate rod and
   piston rod to check the condition of the alignment spigots. If worn change
   them out.
19. Line up the piston with the liner and crank the pump to push the piston into
   the liner with the crosshead extension rod.
20. Once the piston has been inserted into the liner to its full extent, continue
   cranking the pump to allow the intermediate rod to be inserted.

   21. Line up the intermediate rod to the crosshead extension rod and crank the
     pump until the intermediate rod is snug against the piston rod.
   22. Clean and inspect the piston rod clamps for clamps for cracking and coat
      the inside of the clamps with grease.
   23. Install the intermediate rod and piston rod clamps and torque the bolts to
      (12-P-160: 150 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 75 ft-lbs).
   24. Clean out the liner wash tank and refill with clean drill water.
   25. Install the cooling water shroud and hose.
   26. Remove the crank handle and reinstall the side plate.
   27. Replace the piston rod chamber covers.
   28. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   29. Sign off the PTW.
   30. Electrically de-isolate the pump.
   31. Remove all mechanical isolations.
   32. Prime the pump and close the bleed off valve.
   33. Inform the driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps.
   34. Line up the standpipe manifold.
   35. Remove the “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign from the mud pump
       control in the DCR.
   36. Continue circulating with the pumps as required.

Post Task Actions:

    1. With the mud pump running, check  all parts are functioning correctly,
      check for leaks through the weep holes and seals etc.
    2. Ensure the liner wash and lube oil pumps are running.
    3. Clean and tidy away all tools. Bleed off air and coil away airlines.
    4. Dispose of the old liner and piston.
    5. Prepare a new liner and piston.
    6. Inform the storekeeper which parts have been used.
    7. Update the pump books.
    8. Clean and inspect any lifting gear used for damage. Return equipment
      used to the rigging loft reporting any damage.
    9. Review the procedure and JSA.

1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
    is zero pressure on the pump.
2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
3. Clean down the piston rod chamber before removing the liner cooling
   shroud and hose.
4. Remove the side plate and manually crank the pump until the piston is
   almost fully retracted. Loosen and remove the intermediate rod and piston
   rod clamps.
5. Continue cranking the pump until there is sufficient clearance to remove
   the intermediate rod.
6. Continue cranking the pump to extend the crosshead extension rod onto
   the piston rod. Loosely install the piston rod clamp and continue cranking
   the pump to withdraw the piston.
7. Allow the mud to drain from the liner and wash out the piston rod chamber.

    liner and hoist it out of the piston rod chamber.

    multiplier. Install the pilot bushing puller and crank the pump to remove the
    pilot bushing.

13. Clean the module seal groove and wear plate recess.

15. Install a new module seal and wear plate.

   them up to (12-P-160: 780 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 280 ft-lbs).
17. Clean the tell tale hole.
18. Ensure the new liner  is clean and paint has been removed from the
   locating rings.

    front of the pump.

    pilot bushing. Check the seal and make sure it is still located in the liner lip.

   grease.

   seal remains in position).
23. Install the liner clamp and tighten the bolts. Torque them up to the required
   torque (12-P-160: 250 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 100 ft-lbs).
24. Take the new, previously prepared piston and apply grease to the rubber.
   Check the piston is the correct size for the liner in use.

 4.2.6.5 Changing a Suction Module

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of changing a mud pump
suction module.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing a mud pump suction module.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman / Electrician

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedures and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW.
    3. Shut down the pumps.
    4. Close the stand pipe and suction valves. Open the bleed off line and pump
      through to confirm the line is not plugged. Close the suction valves and
     open the suction bleed line.
    5. Continue circulating with the remaining pumps.
    6.  Isolate the pump electrically and mechanically.
    7. While the pump is being isolated, gather tools and equipment required.
      Equipment for changing a suction module:
       •  Seat puller complete with hydraulic pump, Driver for the valve seat,
          flogging bar, sledge hammer, soapy water and brush. Impact wrench
        and socket, chain hoists and slings.
    8. Inspect all hydraulic hoses, pumps and fittings prior to use.
    9. Refer to electrical isolation procedure.
   10. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.
   11. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   12. If any lifting gear is required for the task, check it is fit for purpose, colour
      coded and the certification is valid.
   13. Place a “Do not operate: isolated equipment” sign on mud pump controls in
      the DCR (Driller’s control room).


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               209


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 210 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

Operation:

    1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
        is zero pressure on the pump.
    2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
    3. Use the flogging bar to loosen the valve cap but leave it hand tight in the
      threaded ring. Watch for pinch points.
    4. Remove the bolts joining the suction module onto the suction manifold.
    5. Remove the  six nuts holding the suction module onto the discharge
      module.  If possible use an impact wrench or a hammer wrench and
      sledgehammer. Use a piece of rope on the hammer wrench to prevent  it
      from bouncing on the nut.
    6.  Insert a sling through the holes in the top of the valve cap and attach two
      chain hoists. The chain hoists should rigged to pad eyes where possible
      above and in front of the module.
    7. Using the chain blocks work the module off the ling studs. Once it is off lift
            it up into the sack room for disposal.
    8. Prepare the new module. Thoroughly clean the mating face. Pay particular
       attention the sealing faces and the valve pocket. Check the dowel pin
      holes are clear.
    9.  Install the threaded ring onto the new module and make the bolts up to
      (12-P-160: 2400 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 1775 ft-lbs) making sure  it is correctly
       located.
   10. Thoroughly clean the mating face of the discharge module. Inspect around
     and in the portal for any cracks or washes.
   11. Re-position the chain hoists to install the module on the pump.
   12. Clean and lubricate the six long studs. If any of the studs backed out of the
      discharge module remove the nut and install them a nut and lock nut to
       tighten them in (not a pipe wrench).
   13. Remove the face seal from the discharge module, thoroughly clean the
      groove and install a new one. Check the seal on the suction manifold.
      Replace   if necessary. Check  the  locating dowels are  in place and
     undamaged.
   14. Lift the new module and slide  it over the studs until  it mates with the
      discharge module. Ensure that the bottom of the suction module does not
      drag the seal out of the suction manifold. It may be necessary to lower the
       suction manifold by loosening the bolts holding  it onto the other suction
      modules.
   15. Lubricate and install the six nuts. Tighten them up working on diagonally
      opposing nuts.
   16. Torque the nuts up to (12-P-160: 400 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 300 ft-lbs + 1/3 turn).
   17. Lubricate and insert the suction manifold bolts. If other bolts were loosened
     remember to tighten them.
   18. Clean  the  valve  seating  area  thoroughly,  inspect and ensure   it  is
      thoroughly dry.
   19. Clean and dry the new valve seat.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               210


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 211 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   20. Place the seat into the module and tap lightly using the seat driver. Check
      the seat is sitting level in the seat. Re-install the driver and strike firmly
       three times. Pull the driver and check the seat is properly seated in the
      module. Run a finger under the seat and check gap. This should be approx
     ¼” or less.
   21. Install a new valve and the spring.
   22. Check the seal area in the module and on the valve retainer. Install a new
      valve retainer seal and insert the valve retainer.
   23. Clean and grease the threads on the valve cap, screw into the threaded
        ring. Tighten using the flogging bar.
   24. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   25. Sign off the PTW.
   26. Electrically de-isolate the pump.
   27. Remove all mechanical isolations.
   28. Prime the pump and close the bleed off valve.
   29. Inform the driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps.
   30. Line up the standpipe manifold.
   31. Remove the “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign from the mud pump
       control in the DCR (drillers control room).
   32. Continue circulating with the pumps as required.

Post Task Actions:

    1. With the mud pump running, check  all parts are functioning correctly,
      check for leaks through the weep holes and seals etc.
    2. Once the pump has been running, at first opportunity check tightness of
      valve cap.
    3. Clean and return all tools to proper storage area.
    4. Remove the valve cap, valve retainer, spring, valve and threaded ring,
      from the old module.
    5. Dispose of the module etc.
    6. Prepare a new suction module, install the threaded ring and torque up the
        bolts. Grease and cover all sealing surfaces etc.
    7. Inform the storekeeper which parts have been used.
    8. Update pump books.
    9. Review the procedure and JSA.

4.2.6.6  Changing a Discharge Module

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of changing a mud pump
discharge module.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing the discharge module.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendant / Toolpusher / A/D / Derrickman / Electrician

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedures and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW.
    3. Shut down the pumps.
    4. Close the stand pipe and suction valves. Open the bleed off line and pump
      through to confirm the line is not plugged. Close the suction valves and
     open the suction bleed line.
    5. Continue circulating with the remaining pumps.
    6.  Electrically isolate the pump.
    7. Tag or lock off all valves and switches.
    8. While the pump is being isolated, gather tools and equipment required.
      Equipment for changing a suction module:
       •  Flogging bar, sledge hammer, soapy water and brush. Impact wrench
        and socket, chain hoists and slings
    9. Inspect all hydraulic hoses, pumps and fittings prior to use.
   10. Refer to electrical isolation procedure.
   11. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.
   12. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   13. If any lifting gear is required for the task, check it is fit for purpose, colour
      coded and the certification is valid.
   14. Place a “Do not operate: isolated equipment” sign on the mud pump
       controls in the DCR (Driller’s control room).


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               212


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 213 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   15. Clean and prepare a new discharge module. Thoroughly clean all mating
      faces and sealing areas inside and out. Install a new threaded ring and
      torque up the bolts. Install a new valve seat, valve, valve retainer and valve
      cap.
   16. Install the suction and discharge module retaining studs. Use a nut and
       lock nut to tighten them not a pipe wrench. Install a discharge connector
     and spacer flange as required.
   17. Clean and prepare the mating faces on the suction module and on the
     pump frame. Check the locating dowels. Clean up all the studs, nuts and
       bolts ready for use.

Operation:

To change out the discharge module, the suction module, piston, liner and wear
plate must all be removed first.  It is assumed in this procedure that the user will
follow the instructions in their entirety. This procedure is solely for the removal
and refitting of the discharge module and is not a substitute for the procedures
given elsewhere  in the manual  for removal and  refitting of the components
mentioned above.

Removing the Suction Module:

    1. Ensure the pump has been mechanically and electrically isolated and there
        is zero pressure on the pump.
    2. Attempt to operate the pump to confirm the isolation.
    3. Use the flogging bar to loosen the valve cap but leave it hand tight in the
      threaded ring. Watch for pinch points.
    4. Remove the bolts joining the suction module onto the suction manifold.
    5. Remove the  six nuts holding the suction module onto the discharge
      module.  If possible use an impact wrench or a hammer wrench and
      sledgehammer. Use a piece of rope on the hammer wrench to prevent  it
      from bouncing on the nut.
    6.  Insert a sling through the holes in the top of the valve cap and attach two
      chain hoists. The chain hoists should rigged to pad eyes where possible
      above and in front of the module.
    7. Using the chain blocks work the module off the ling studs. Once it is off lift
            it up into the sack room for disposal.




Removing the Piston, Liner and Wear Plate:

    1. Clean down the piston rod chamber before removing the liner cooling
      shroud and hose.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               213


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 214 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    2. Remove the side plate and manually crank the pump until the piston is
      almost fully retracted. Loosen and remove the intermediate rod and piston
      rod clamps.
    3. Continue cranking the pump until there is sufficient clearance to remove
      the intermediate rod.
    4. Continue cranking the pump to extend the crosshead extension rod onto
      the piston rod. Loosely install the piston rod clamp and continue cranking
      the pump to withdraw the piston.
    5. Allow the mud to drain from the liner and wash out the piston rod chamber.
    6. Loosen and remove the liner clamp bolts. Re-insert the bolts, one through
      each side of the clamp as handles. Tap the clamp to remove it.
    7.  Insert the wedge tool between the pilot bushing and the liner to loosen the
        liner. Choke a small canvas or steel sling (1/2 ton) around the liner and use
     a chain block and the trolley beam on the front of the pump to remove the
        liner and hoist it out of the piston rod chamber.
    8. Remove the pilot bushing retaining bolts with an impact wrench or torque
        multiplier. Install the pilot bushing puller and crank the pump to remove the
        pilot bushing.

Removing the Discharge Module:

    1. Remove the discharge connector retaining bolts retaining the half flange
      washers and retract the connector.
    2. Depending on the position of the damaged module remove the mud cross
      (discharge strainer block) or the relief valve standpipe flange as required.
    3. Loosen the discharge module retaining bolts in the piston rod chamber.
    4.  Insert a sling through the holes in the top of the valve cap and attach two
      chain hoists. The chain hoists should be rigged to pad eyes where possible
      above and in front of the module.
    5. Work the module off the dowel pins and remove  it. Lift  it up to the sack
       store for stripping down and disposal.
    6. Remove the wear plate.
    7. Remove the discharge connector from the module, clean the mating
      surfaces and  install new o rings. Grease the faces and re-instate the
      connector into the module pushing  it in far enough to clear the adjacent
      module.



Installing Discharge Module:

    1. Clean up the mating surfaces on the mud pump.
    2.  Lift the discharge module and enter the studs into the pump frame. Slide
      the module  into  position,  lubricate the studs and  install the module
       retaining nuts (hand tight).


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               214


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 215 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    3. Remove the sling and chain hoists from the discharge module and rig up to
            lift the suction module.
    4.  Install new suction module and suction manifold seals and  install the
     module as per the next procedure.
    5.  Fully tighten the discharge module to the pump frame (12-P-160:1460 ft-
       lbs) (8-P-80: 780 ft-lbs), the suction module to the discharge module (12-P-
      160: 400 ft-lbs + 1/3 turn) (8-P-80: 300 ft-lbs + 1/3 turn) and the suction
     module to the suction manifold (12-P-160:400 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 380 ft-lbs).
    6. Ensure the o ring on the discharge connectors are in position and extend
      the connectors. Install the half flange washers and bolts.
    7.  Install a new module seal and wear plate, re-instate the pilot bushing and
      torque the bolts (12-P-160:780 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 280 ft-lbs).
    8. Remove the valve cap from the discharge module and install the valve
       seat, valve, spring, valve retainer and valve cap if not done previously.

Installling the Suction Module:

    1. Prepare the new module. Thoroughly clean the mating face. Pay particular
       attention the sealing faces and the valve pocket. Check the dowel pin
      holes are clear.
    2.  Install the threaded ring onto the new module and make the bolts up to 12-
      P-160: 2400 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 1775 ft-lbs), making sure it is correctly located.
    3. Thoroughly clean the mating face of the discharge module. Inspect around
     and in the portal for any cracks or washes.
    4. Re-position the chain hoists to install the module on the pump.
    5. Clean and lubricate the six long studs. If any of the studs backed out of the
      discharge module remove the nut and install them a nut and lock nut to
       tighten them in (not a pipe wrench).
    6. Remove the face seal from the discharge module, thoroughly clean the
      groove and install a new one. Check the seal on the suction manifold.
      Replace   if necessary. Check  the  locating dowels are  in place and
     undamaged.
    7.  Lift the new module and slide  it over the studs until  it mates with the
      discharge module. Ensure that the bottom of the suction module does not
      drag the seal out of the suction manifold. It may be necessary to lower the
       suction manifold by loosening the bolts holding  it onto the other suction
      modules.
    8. Lubricate and install the six nuts. Tighten them up working on diagonally
      opposing nuts.
    9. Torque the nuts up to (12-P-160: 400 ft-lbs + 1/3 turn) (8-P-80: 300 ft-lbs +
         1/3 turn).
   10. Lubricate and insert the suction manifold bolts. If other bolts were loosened
     remember to tighten them.
   11. Clean  the  valve  seating  area  thoroughly,  inspect and ensure   it  is
      thoroughly dry.
   12. Clean and dry the new valve seat.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               215


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 216 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   13. Place the seat into the module and tap lightly using the seat driver. Check
      the seat is sitting level in the seat. Re-install the driver and strike firmly
       three times. Pull the driver and check the seat is properly seated in the
      module. Run a finger under the seat and check gap. This should be approx
     ¼” or less.
   14. Install a new valve and the spring.
   15. Check the seal area in the module and on the valve retainer. Install a new
      valve retainer seal and insert the valve retainer.
   16. Clean and grease the threads on the valve cap, screw into the threaded
        ring. Tighten using the flogging bar.

Installing the Piston, Liner and Wear Plate:

    1. Ensure the new liner  is clean and paint has been removed from the
       locating rings.
    2. Clean the module seal groove and wear plate recess.
    3. Clean the inside and outside locating rings on the pilot bushing with emery,
      as required. Clean the retaining stud threads.
    4.  Install a new module seal and wear plate.
    5.  Install the pilot bushing, grease the studs, install the retaining nuts torque
     them up to (12-P-160: 780 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 280 ft-lbs).
    6. Clean the tell tale hole.
    7. Lubricate the locating rings on the liner with copper coat and insert it into
       retaining ring. Check the seal and make sure  it is still located in the liner
         lip.
    8. Clean and inspect the liner clamp and coat the inside of the clamp with
      grease.
    9. Slide the liner into the pilot bushing and push firmly home (check the liner
       seal remains in position).
   10. Install the liner clamp and tighten the bolts. Torque them up to the required
      torque (12-P-160: 250 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 100 ft-lbs).
   11. Take the new, previously prepared piston and apply grease to the rubber.
     Check the piston is the correct size for the liner in use.
   12. Inspect the ends of the crosshead extension rod, intermediate rod and
       piston rod to check the condition of the alignment spigots. If worn change
     them out.
   13. Line up the piston with the liner and crank the pump to push the piston into
      the liner with the crosshead extension rod.
   14. Once the piston has been inserted into the liner to its full extent, continue
      cranking the pump to allow the intermediate rod to be inserted.
   15. Line up the intermediate rod to the crosshead extension rod and crank the
     pump until the intermediate rod is snug against the piston rod.
   16. Clean and inspect the piston rod clamps for clamps for cracking and coat
      the inside of the clamps with grease.
   17. Install the intermediate rod and piston rod clamps and torque the bolts to
      (12-P-160:150 ft-lbs) (8-P-80: 75 ft-lbs).

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               216


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 217 از 286 ]

                         Issue Date

                                          Document Title:

                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and

   18. Clean out the liner wash tank and refill with clean drill water.
   19. Remove all tools and equipment from the pump before commencing de-
        isolation.
   20. Sign off the PTW.
   21. Electrically de-isolate the pump.
   22. Remove all mechanical isolations.
   23. Prime the pump and close the bleed off valve.
   24. Inform the driller the pump is ready and to stop the pumps.
   25. Line up the standpipe manifold.
   26. Remove the “Do not operate, equipment isolated” sign from the mud pump
       control in the DCR (drillers control room).
   27. Continue circulating with the pumps as required.

Post Task Actions:

    1. With the mud pump running, check  all parts are functioning correctly,
      check for leaks through the weep holes and seals etc.
    2. Ensure the liner wash and lube oil pumps are running.
    3.  After an initial run in period, re-check the torque on all the nuts and bolts to
      the correct value.
    4. Clean and tidy away all tools. Bleed off air and coil away airlines.
    5.  Strip down and dispose of the old discharge module.
    6. Prepare a new discharge module.
    7. Inform the storekeeper which parts have been used.
    8. Update the pumps books.
    9. Clean and inspect any lifting gear used for damage. Return equipment
      used to the rigging loft reporting any damage.
   10. Review the procedure and JSA.
