 4.2.6.7 Changing and Charging a Pulsation Dampener

Purpose:

The purpose  of  this procedure  is  to control the operation  of changing the
pulsation dampener.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during changing the pulsation dampener.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Senior Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman / Electrician

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW.
    3. Inform the Driller before working on the mud pump.
    4. Check the availability of any spares or replacement parts that may be
       required.
    5. Assemble at the worksite all the necessary tools and equipment.
    6. Ensure that  all tools and equipment are in good condition and  fit for
      purpose.
    7.  Electrically and mechanically isolate the pump.
    8. Ensure zero pressure on the pump, close the standpipe valves and open
      the bleed-off valve. Close the suction line valves.
    9. De-assign the pump to be repaired and place a “Do not use  / pump
       isolated” sign on the pump controls.
   10. Ensure there is adequate lighting in the work area.
   11. In addition to mandatory personnel protective equipment, ear protection is
       to be worn.
   12. Check gas charging valve assembly and nitrogen bottles are available in
      the mud pump room.
   13. Refer to manufacturers dampener charging and replacing procedures as
      per manual.

Operation:


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               218


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 219 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Issue Date
                                                      Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                      January 2011
                     Revision No.
                                        Manual Nr:
                           0
                     Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                       Document Title:
                                                                 Procedures Manual
                    Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                    K.Tiersma                          Approved by

1. Drain down the discharge manifold through the drain valve or by removing
   a discharge valve.
2. Remove the protective gauge cover and confirm the pre-charge pressure
    is at zero. Caution – if the pressure gauge reads zero this does not mean
   there  is no pressure in bladder. Remove the pipe plug and open the
   dampener gas valve slowly – if pressure is evident the gas gauge could be
    faulty. If so bleed off the gas, replace the gauge and charge the dampener
    to 750psi with Nitrogen.
3.  If there is no pressure evident remove the gas charging valve assembly
   and pressure gauge.
4. Remove the nuts from the dampener top plate.
5.  Refit the protective gauge cover which is used as a lifting device (ensure
    this has been inspected during lifting gear inspection) for the top cover
    plate.
6.  Install jacking bolts in the top cover and jack it clear of the shell. Remove
   the cover plate using the overhead lifting beam or pad eye and chain hoist.
  Remove the jacking bolts.
7. Remove the bolt from the stabiliser and fit an eye bolt of the correct size.
   Hook the chain host onto the eye bolt and remove the stabiliser.
8.  Install the eye bolt into the diaphragm insert and remove the diaphragm
   using overhead lifting beam and chain hoist.
9. Clean and inspect inside the shell of the dampener for corrosion and
   erosion particularly at the neck in the bottom. If heavily corroded then ultra
   sonic testing should be carried out to ascertain the wall thickness.
10. Lubricate the outside of the new diaphragm with water only. Fold the
   bladder to get it through the opening and push it into the dampener shell,
   then open  it out into the shell. Install the eyebolt and slowly work the
   bladder up to the neck of the shell to engage the lip of the bladder into the
   groove of the shell.
11. Once the bladder is fully seated install the stabiliser.
12. Refit the cover plate to the top of the dampener and torque the studs to the
   recommended value.
13. Remove the protective gauge cover and  refit the gas charging valve
   assembly. Ensure thread sealant is used on the valve assembly threads.
14. Hook up the charging hose from the nitrogen bottle to the gauge assembly
   on the dampener.
15. Charge the dampener to 750psi with Nitrogen by opening the gas charging
   valve on the dampener and the nitrogen bottle valve.
16. Periodic checks of the gas pressure in the dampener can be carried out by
   closing off the nitrogen bottle valve and reading the gauge pressure at the
   dampener.
17. Once the dampener is charged to the required pressure, close off the
   nitrogen bottle and dampener valves, rig down the gas charging hose from
   the nitrogen bottle. (Be aware of possible trapped pressure in the hose
   when rigging it down).


                Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               219


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 220 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   18. Refit the pipe plug to the charging assembly on the dampener,  refit
       protective gauge cover.
   19. Re-check the nitrogen pre-charge pressure after a 1hour period top up as
       required.
   20. Reinstate the mud pump discharge valve if it was removed.
   21. Sign off the PTW.
   22. On completion of task remove the electrical and mechanical isolations.
     Remove the “Do not use / pump isolated” sign from the pump controls and
      assign power to the pump.
   23. Assign the pump to local control.
   24. Open the suction valves and the bleed off valve. Prime the pump with the
      charge pump until good flow is seen from the bleed line.
   25. Run the pump slowly to ensure all air is removed.
   26. Close the bleed  off valve and open the standpipe valves up to the
      standpipe manifold. Pressure test the dampener to the highest pressure
       possible (limited by the liner size and relief valve settings) against the rig
        floor manifold so pressure can be bled off through the choke.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clear the worksite of tools and equipment.
    2. Record the pressure in the nitrogen bottles, replace as required.
    3. Update the mud pump record book.
    4. Close any work permits raised for the job.
    5. Review the procedure and JSA.

4.2.7 High Pressure Testing


4.2.7.1  High Pressure Testing Operations

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of high pressure testing.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during high pressure testing operations.

Responsible Persons:

OIM / Rig Superintendent /Toolpusher / Driller / A/D / Derrickman

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1.  All high pressure testing is covered by the permit to work system. Prior to
      pressure testing a PTW (permit to work) and JSA (Job Safety Analysis) will
     be raised and signed by the OIM. Pressure testing is defined as applying
      pressure to a sealed item of equipment for the purpose of testing the
        integrity.
    2. The permit must describe the equipment to be pressure tested and its
       location on the rig. It is the duty of the permit controller and the persons
       involved in the task to check that the testing does not clash with other
       operations.
    3. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure, PTW and JSA.
    4. Upon receipt of the permit, the rig site copy must be displayed at the work
        site, with the equipment testing procedure and JSA.
    5. The equipment testing procedure and JSA should also be displayed at the
      pressure test pump.
    6. Check the calibration and test certificates for the high-pressure pump unit
       to be used.
    7. Ensure there is a PRV (pressure relief valve) on the test pump. Check the
        rating.
    8. The Senior Tool Pusher will check the PRV certification and that  it is set
       within the MWP (maximum working pressure) of the lowest rated piece of
      equipment.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               221


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 222 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    9. Ensure all pumps in the system to be tested have been electrically and
      mechanically isolated.  All local and remote controls  will have “Do not
       operate: isolated equipment” signs displayed.
   10. Ensure any test pressure does not exceed the maximum working pressure
       of the equipment to be tested. Verify that the equipment to be tested is in a
       suitable condition to be tested and is fit for purpose.
   11. All temporary lines must be snubbed to suitable fastenings.
   12. All barriers will be in place and checked, to keep non essential personnel
     away from test area.
   13. The pressure testing medium will normally be water (seawater or fresh). If
      any other medium is used ensure  it is safe to use under pressure and
       additional  procedures  are  in  place. Use  additional PPE  (personal
       protective equipment) as required.

Operation:

    1. Ensure a permit to work has been made out for the task. This must be
      checked at shift changes.
    2. Ensure barriers are in place to prevent non essential personnel entering
      the area. The pressure path from the test pump to the equipment to be
       tested must also be barriered as required.
    3. Check all the connections have been correctly made up and the pressure
       lines have been snubbed to a suitable anchorage.
    4. Ensure there is a relief path, should the pressure pass the equipment
      being tested.
    5. Make a P.A announcement that pressure testing is about to commence.
      This announcement must specify the area the testing is taking place and
         all associated areas.
    6.  Instructions between the test pump operator and the equipment being
       tested must be clear and concise and there must be open  lines  of
      communication i.e. Radios or telephone.
    7. The instructions given to the test pump operator must include the line up
      being employed, the equipment to be pressure tested and the pressure to
     be applied. It is important to specify the above items as it enables the test
     pump operator to annotate the test chart correctly and avoids confusion.
    8. The test chart must be suitable for both low and high pressure tests (two
      pens or two chart recorders).
    9. Flush the line to confirm the correct line up. If any above normal pressure
        is seen, the flushing will be shut down.
   10. Commence pressure testing.
   11. Apply a low pressure test to the test pump and pressure line against a
      closed valve.
   12. When applying the low pressure test do not increase the pressure above
      the test pressure and bleed down.
   13. The low pressure test will be to 200-300 psi and following stabilization a 5
      minute straight line on the calibrated test chart must be achieved as per

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               222


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 223 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

      API RP53 although this may be extended to ensure a straight line  is
      achieved.
   14. Following the low pressure test increase the pressure at a steady rate to
      apply the high pressure test.
   15. Do not apply a pressure greater than the required test pressure and if the
      pressure drops increase it back up to the test pressure.
   16. Following stabilization a 5 minute straight line on the calibrated test chart
      must be achieved as per API RP53 although this may be extended to
      ensure a straight line is achieved.
   17. In the event of a leak always bleed the pressure off and get this confirmed
      by the test pump operator. Do not work on or attempt to tighten any fittings
       that are under pressure.
   18. Make hourly PA announcements during the pressure testing operations.
      This practice ensures that all personnel, not involved with the operation are
      informed of the ongoing hazard.
   19. At the conclusion of each test, bleed the pressure off, and confirm before
        lining up for the next test
   20. If any equipment is to be tested off the critical path, i.e. stabbing valves or
      Gray valves, this should be carried out using the Sub Sea test pump in an
      area away from rig floor if possible. This area should be clear of any other
      equipment.
   21. Testing will be carried out in the same manner as in the above procedure.

Post Task Actions:

    1. When all pressure testing has been completed ensure all the lines are bled
     down to zero before any rigging down.
    2. At the conclusion  of  all testing make a PA announcement informing
      personnel that the high pressure testing is complete.
    3. Rig down  all equipment and remove  all  barriers placed  to  prevent
      personnel access.
    4. Any machinery isolated for the testing will be de-isolated.
    5. Review the procedure and JSA.
    6. Tidy up all tools and rig down any lines.
    7. Sign off and return PTW to permit control area.

 4.2.7.2 Pressure Testing the Choke Manifold

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of pressure testing the
Choke Manifold.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the pressure testing of the Choke Manifold. It ensures that all the valves
within the choke manifold are pressure tested to the required pressure and from
both sides where necessary.

Responsible Persons:

OIM / Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / Sub Sea Engineer / A/D

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1.  All high pressure testing is covered by the permit to work system. Prior to
      pressure testing a PTW (permit to work) and JSA (Job Safety Analysis) will
     be raised and signed by the OIM. Pressure testing is defined as applying
      pressure to a sealed item of equipment for the purpose of testing the
        integrity.
    2. The permit must describe the equipment to be pressure tested and its
       location on the rig. It is the duty of the permit controller and the persons
       involved in the task to check that the testing does not clash with other
       operations.
    3. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure, PTW and JSA.
    4. Upon receipt of the permit, the rig site copy must be displayed at the work
        site, with the equipment testing procedure and JSA.
    5. The equipment testing procedure and JSA should also be displayed at the
      pressure test pump.
    6. Check the calibration and test certificates for the high-pressure pump unit
       to be used.
    7. Ensure there is a PRV (pressure relief valve) on the test pump. Check the
        rating.
    8. The Rig Superintendent will check the PRV certification and that  it is set
       within the MWP (maximum working pressure) of the lowest rated piece of
      equipment.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               224


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 225 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    9. Ensure all pumps in the system to be tested have been electrically and
      mechanically isolated.  All local and remote controls  will have “Do not
       operate: isolated equipment” signs displayed.
   10. Ensure any test pressure does not exceed the maximum working pressure
       of the equipment to be tested. Verify that the equipment to be tested is in a
       suitable condition to be tested and is fit for purpose.
   11. All temporary lines must be snubbed to suitable fastenings.
   12. All barriers will be in place and checked, to keep non essential personnel
     away from test area.
   13. The pressure testing medium will normally be water (seawater or fresh). If
      any other medium is used ensure  it is safe to use under pressure and
       additional  procedures  are  in  place. Use  additional PPE  (personal
       protective equipment) as required.

Method:

    1. This test procedure can only test the choke and kill isolation valves from
      above. These valves must be pressure tested from below while pressure
       testing the BOP and are included in procedure 4.2.7.3 – Pressure Testing
      the BOP Subsea.
    2.  Prior to pressure testing thoroughly grease the choke manifold valves.
    3. Thoroughly flush the choke manifold to remove any trapped air. Further
       flushing may be required for pressure testing the choke downstream and
       buffer tank valves.
    4. Ensure there is a leak path through the standpipe manifold while pressure
       testing the choke manifold to 10,000psi.
    5. The choke bodies will be tested to 10,000psi but the chokes themselves
         will not be pressure tested.

Test Sequence:

    1. Line the cement unit up to the choke manifold, entering the choke manifold
       at valve 1 and carry out tests 1 to 6.
    2. Pressure Tests 1 to 6  will be to 250psi and 10,000psis for 5 and 10
      minutes respectively.
    3. When test 6 is completed, line up the cement unit to the cement manifold
     and across to the choke manifold, entering at valve 4.
    4. Tests 6 to 12  will be to 250psi and 10,000psi for 5 and 10 minutes
       respectively.
    5. Test 13  will be to 250 psi and 5000psi  for 5 and 10 minutes
      respectively.

 4.2.7.3 Pressure Testing the BOP Subsea

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of pressure testing the
BOP Subsea.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the pressure testing of the BOP.

Responsible Persons:

OIM / Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / A/D

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1.  All high pressure testing is covered by the permit to work system. Prior to
      pressure testing a PTW (permit to work) and JSA (Job Safety Analysis) will
     be raised and signed by the OIM. Pressure testing is defined as applying
      pressure to a sealed item of equipment for the purpose of testing the
        integrity.
    2. The permit must describe the equipment to be pressure tested and its
       location on the rig. It is the duty of the permit controller and the persons
       involved in the task to check that the testing does not clash with other
       operations.
    3. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure, PTW and JSA.
    4. Upon receipt of the permit, the rig site copy must be displayed at the work
        site, with the equipment testing procedure and JSA.
    5. The equipment testing procedure and JSA should also be displayed at the
      pressure test pump.
    6. Check the calibration and test certificates for the high-pressure pump unit
       to be used.
    7. Ensure there is a PRV (pressure relief valve) on the test pump. Check the
        rating.
    8. The Senior Tool Pusher will check the PRV certification and that  it is set
       within the MWP (maximum working pressure) of the lowest rated piece of
      equipment.
    9. Thoroughly flush the kill and choke lines before pressure testing.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               239


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 240 از 286 ]

   10. Ensure all pumps in the system to be tested have been electrically and
      mechanically isolated.  All local and remote controls  will have “Do not
       operate: isolated equipment” signs displayed.
   11. Ensure any test pressure does not exceed the maximum working pressure
       of the equipment to be tested. Verify that the equipment to be tested is in a
       suitable condition to be tested and is fit for purpose.
   12. All temporary lines must be snubbed to suitable fastenings.
   13. All barriers will be in place and checked, to keep non essential personnel
     away from test area.

Operation:

    1. The pressure testing medium will normally be water (seawater or fresh). If
      any other medium is used ensure it is safe to use under pressure and
       additional procedures are in place. Use additional PPE (personal
       protective equipment) as required.
    2.  Prior to pressure testing the BOP, the Choke manifold should be pressure
       tested as per procedure 4.2.7.2 – Pressure Testing the Choke Manifold.
    3. Pressure test the kill and choke lines and shear rams (if required) while
     RIH with the BOP test tool.
    4.  Pull the wear nominal seat protector if required and run the BOP test tool
      run in on drill pipe and set it in the wellhead.
    5. The following procedures will be used when testing down the kill and
      choke lines:

 4.2.7.4 Pressure Testing the Diverter

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of pressure testing the
Diverter.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the pressure testing of the diverter. It ensures that all the valves within the
riser and diverter are pressure tested to the required pressure.

Responsible Persons:

OIM / Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / Sub Sea Engineer / A/D

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1.  All pressure testing  is covered by the permit to work system. Prior to
      pressure testing a PTW (permit to work) and JSA (Job Safety Analysis) will
     be raised and signed by the OIM. Pressure testing is defined as applying
      pressure to a sealed item of equipment for the purpose of testing the
        integrity.
    2. The permit must describe the equipment to be pressure tested and its
       location on the rig. It is the duty of the permit controller and the persons
       involved in the task to check that the testing does not clash with other
       operations.
    3. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure, PTW and JSA.
    4. Upon receipt of the permit, the rig site copy must be displayed at the work
        site, with the equipment testing procedure and JSA.
    5. The equipment testing procedure and JSA should also be displayed at the
      pressure test pump.
    6. Check the calibration and test certificates for the high-pressure pump unit
       to be used.
    7. Ensure there is a PRV (pressure relief valve) on the test pump. Check the
        rating.
    8. The Rig Superintendent will check the PRV certification and that  it is set
       within the MWP (maximum working pressure) of the lowest rated piece of
      equipment.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               261


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 262 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    9. Ensure all pumps in the system to be tested have been electrically and
      mechanically isolated.  All local and remote controls  will have “Do not
       operate: isolated equipment” signs displayed.
   10. Ensure any test pressure does not exceed the maximum working pressure
       of the equipment to be tested. Verify that the equipment to be tested is in a
       suitable condition to be tested and is fit for purpose.
   11. All temporary lines must be snubbed to suitable fastenings.
   12. All barriers will be in place and checked, to keep non essential personnel
     away from test area.
   13. The pressure testing medium will normally be water (seawater or fresh).
     Use additional PPE (personal protective equipment) as required.

Method:

    1. Run the BOP test tool, and install the diverter packer. Pressure test the
    BOP as per procedure 4.2.7.3 – Pressure Testing the BOP Subsea.
    2. Ensure the diverter and diverter packer lock down dogs are locked.
    3. Function test the diverter control system, riser  fill valve and riser booster
       valves.
    4. Close the hole  fill line valve, trip tank return line valve, the shaker valve
     and the port and starboard overboard valves.
    5. Line up the cement unit to pump down the Kill line, open the upper  kill
        failsafe valves and fill the riser to the diverter packer.
    6. Close the diverter packer and pressure test the riser and diverter to 500psi
        for 10mins with the cement unit.
    7. Check the telescopic joint packer for leakage while pressuring up the
      system.
    8. Bleed off the pressure.
    9. Ensure the pressure is zero and open the diverter packer.
   10. Close the kill failsafe valves.
   11. Open the diverter valves.
   12. Unlock and remove the diverter packer and pull the BOP test tool.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Line up the choke manifold for drilling.
    2. Run the nominal seat protector if required.
    3. Service the BOP test tool.

4.2.8 Rotary Table


 4.2.8.1 Pulling the Master Bushings

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the removal and installation of the
Insert and Master Bushings with pipe in the hole.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during pulling or inserting the master bushings.

Responsible Persons:

Toolpusher / Driller / Assistant Driller / Drill Crew

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Check the wire on the air winch to be used is evenly and tightly spooled
      onto the air winch drum.
    3. Position the travelling block as high up the derrick as possible and keep
      the string stationary while the bushings are removed and inserted.
    4. The single legged and master bushing pullers are to be inspected and
        certified for lifting. Check if fit for purpose.
    5. Ensure the area around the rotary table is clear of tools and equipment
       that could get knocked down the hole when the bushings are removed.
    6. Remove the cover from the Mousehole.
    7.  Inertia reels and safety harnesses are  to be worn when the Master
      bushings are removed with no diverter in place.
    8. The Assistant Driller with a clear view of the winch operator and the rotary
       table will control the operation.
    9.  If the Master bushings are to be out for a short period of time, gates or
       barriers should be installed.
   10. If the bushings are to be removed for a considerable length of time the
      opening should be covered with scaffolding planks and secured in place.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               263


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 264 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

Pulling the master bushings and bushing inserts:

    1. Attach the single bushing pullers to an air winch. Check the shackles have
       pin retainers installed.
    2. Ensure the air winch wire is not snagged in the derrick.
    3. Remove the master bushing hinge pin by hand (do not use the bushing
       pullers and air winch) and put it down in a safe area. Do not drop or throw
      the hinge pin down on the deck as this can result in damage to the pin.
     (Remove the hinge pin before inserts are removed to reduce potential for
      dropping down hole).
    4. Unlock the insert bushings.
    5. Place the hooks of the single bushing pullers into the holes provided in the
       insert bushing and remove them one at a time.
    6. Holds handles on the bushing pullers and keeping feet clear slowly pick up
      the insert with the banksman controlling the operation.
    7.  Lift the bushing slowly out of the Master bushing, move it clear of the rotary
       table and set it down in an upright position on the deck.
    8.  If there is pipe in the hole ensure the drill crew are clear and turn the rotary
       table to present the other insert bushing for removal.
    9. Remove the second insert bushing as above.
   10. Remove the single bushing pullers and attach the Master bushing pullers
       to the air winch.
   11. Ensure the drill crew are clear and turn the rotary so that the remaining
      hinge pin is facing the mouse hole to enable the master bushings to open
     when lifted out of the rotary table.
   12. Place the four hooks of the Master bushing pullers into the slots provided
       with each pair of hooks to a half of the master bushings. Ensure they are
       pointing outwards and that they are hooked in so that the master bushings
      can open when lifted.
   13. Keeping each of the hooks in place with a boot, take the weight of the
      Master bushings on the air winch and then stand clear.
   14. Hoist the master bushings just clear of the rotary table and then hinge the
      two halves apart, allow them to open taking care not stand in the direction
       of travel as they swing clear of the rotary.
   15. Guide the master bushings to a safe area and keeping feet clear set them
     down squarely on the deck.

Installing the master bushings and bushing inserts:

    1. Check the hooks of the master bushing pullers are securely in place.
    2. Keeping feet well clear hoist the master bushings up off the deck and
      guide them to the rotary table.
    3. Hinge the bushings open enough to allow them to pass around the pipe.
    4. Hinge the bushings closed and lower them slowly into the rotary table.
    5. Turn the master bushings so that the drive lugs line up with their slots.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               264


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 265 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    6. Lower them into place slowly slacking off on the air winch so that the
      bushing puller hooks can be disengaged.
    7. Remove the master bushing pullers from the air winch and attach the
       single bushing pullers.
    8. Keeping feet clear install the first insert bushing and lock it into place.
    9. Ensuring the drill crew keep clear turn the rotary to present the recess for
      the second insert bushing. Insert the second insert bushing and lock it into
       place.
   10. Lubricate the master bushing pin and replace it gently, working the pin up
     and down until  it is fully entered, do not use a hammer to force the pin
     home.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Replace the mouse hole cover.
    2. Remove the bushing pullers from the air winch and hang them up.
    3. Secure the air winch.
    4. Clear away any gates or barriers used.
    5. Review the procedure and JSA.

 4.2.8.2 Pulling the Master Bushing while Riserless

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the removal and installation of the
Insert and Master Bushings while drilling riserless.

Scope:

Whilst drilling top hole sections i.e. 26” hole, it may be necessary at some stages
to remove the Master bushings, leaving rotary open to sea below, during this
operation over side working policies and procedures will apply. This procedure
describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and during pulling or
inserting the master bushings.

Responsible Persons:

Toolpusher / Driller / Assistant Driller / Drill Crew

Procedure:

Safety Precaution and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Raise a PTW (Permit to Work) to meet the requirements for working over
      the side.
    3.  Fall arrestors and safety harnesses and life vests are to be worn when the
      Master bushings are removed with no diverter in place.
    4. Two pad eyes should be installed at the bottom of the dolly tracks.
    5. While drilling riser less two fall arrestors will be installed on the above pad
      eyes.
    6. The two fall arrestors should be in current colour coding and Certification
     and they should also be fit for purpose.
    7. Visual inspection of these fall arrestors should be made and  if the button
     on reel has been activated, then  it must not be used, but set aside for
       return ashore and the Mate informed.
    8. While drilling riser-less the two safety harnesses and life vests should be
       located in the DCR and checked for wear or damage.
    9. Check the wire on the air winch to be used is evenly and tightly spooled
      onto the air winch drum.
   10. Position the travelling block as high up the derrick as possible and keep
      the string stationary while the bushings are removed and inserted.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               266


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 267 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   11. The two hook insert pullers and the four hook master bushing pullers are to
     be inspected and certified for lifting. Check if fit for purpose.
   12. Ensure the area around the rotary table is clear of tools and equipment
       that could get knocked down the hole when the bushings are removed.
   13. Remove the cover from the Mousehole.
   14. The operation will be under the control of the AD who will act as banksman
     and have a clear view of the rotary and the air winch.
   15. If the Master bushings are to be out for a short period of time, gates or
       barriers should be installed.
   16. If the bushings are to be removed for a considerable length of time the
      opening should be covered with scaffolding planks and secured in place.

Pulling the master bushings and bushing inserts:

    1. The master bushings to be pulled as per procedure 4.2.8.1 – Pulling the
      Master Bushings.
    2. Two dedicated personnel to don safety harnesses and life vests with fall
       arrestors attached to the back of the harness.
    3. Only personnel with safety harnesses and fall arrestors attached are to go
      near the rotary when it is open to the sea, there will be no exceptions.
    4. At the earliest opportunity the master bushings should be re-inserted so
       that normal access can be resumed.
    5. No work  will be carried out under the rotary table when the master
      bushings have been removed.

Installing the master bushings and bushing inserts:

Master bushings to be installed as per procedure 4.2.8.1 – Pulling the Master
Bushings

Post Task Actions:

    1. As soon as is practical, install the master bushings.
    2. Sign off the Permit to Work.
    3.  Install the hole cover if there is no pipe in the hole or use the Chinese hole
       cover.
    4. Check the safety harnesses and life jackets for damage.
    5. Replace the mouse hole cover.
    6. Remove the bushing pullers from the air winch and hang them up.
    7. Secure the air winch.
    8. Clear away any gates or barriers used.
    9. Return the safety harness and life vests to the DCR until riser-less drilling
      operations are complete.
   10. If the fall arrestors are to be used again then they can be left in place, but
      the lines should be fully retracted to prevent damage.
    11. Review the procedure and JSA.

 4.2.8.3 Using the Iron Roughneck

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure  is to control the operation of using the iron
roughneck. Full operating instructions for the iron roughneck are given in the
Canrig TM 120 Manual

Scope:

The following procedure describes the required  activities, which need to be
carried out prior to and while using the iron roughneck

Responsible persons:

Rig superintendent / Tool Pusher / Driller / A/D

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety Analysis).

Be aware of High Voltage in the main electrical box and high pressure in the
hoses

A Pre-Operation Inspection should be performed prior to each use and periodically
during extended shifts. Ensure the following are inspected

      •    Check for external oil leaks and repair as necessary. This is extremely
            important.
      •    Check the lubricant level in the spinner gear box. Top up as necessary.
     •   Check hydraulic hose and fittings for damage. Replace chafed or
            deteriorated hoses as necessary.
     •   Check for loose, missing, or corroded bolts, pins, keepers, and cotter
           pins. Replace or tighten as necessary.

Ensure personnel are clear of work area BEFORE operating wrench.

Read all warning and caution tag information provided for safe operation of the
wrench.

Inspect the wrench at the beginning of each work shift. Defects should be
corrected immediately.

An equipment warm-up procedure is recommended for all start-ups and essential
at ambient temperatures below +40°F. (See Warm-up Procedure in the manual)


OPERATION:

Full operating instructions are given in Section 3 of the Canrig TM120 Operating
Manual

MAKE SURE THE ROTARY TABLE IS UNLOCKED DURING WRENCH
OPERATION. FAILURE TO HAVE ROTARY TABLE UNLOCKED COULD
RESULT IN PROPERTY DAMAGE, SEVERE PERSONAL INJURY OR DEATH.

NEVER RUN THE WRENCH OR SPINNER ON COLLAR SPIRALS OR HARD
BANDING!

During operation regularly inspect the wrench dies are clean, secure and in good
condition. Change as required. For full maintenance procedures refer Section 6 of
the TM120 Canrig Operation Manual

Warnings

Never operate the wrench when not centred on the pipe. Premature wear of
the upper and lower tong pivot rings and bushing and rotation drive will
result.

Interlocks

The unit has a number of interlocks installed and the user should be aware of
these

The unit will not retract from Hole Center unless it has been lowered enough to
clear the derrick structure (Carriage unit) or raised enough to clear the floor (Arm
unit) and the Upper Tong Clamp, Lower Tong Clamp, and Spinner Clamp must
be open.

The unit will not extend to Hole Center unless the Upper Tong Clamp, Lower
Tong Clamp, and Spinner Clamp have been opened.

The unit will not lift until it has been moved forward far enough to clear the derrick
structure, and the unit can not be raised or lowered until the spinner clamp and
tong clamps have opened.

The Spinner will not operate unless the unit is positioned at Hole Center.

Check all pivot pins are secure and report any visible wear

4.2.9  Fluids


 4.2.9.1 Cleaning Mud Tanks

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of cleaning the mud
tanks.

Scope:

This procedure describes the activities which are to be followed prior to and
during the cleaning of the mud tanks.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent  / Toolpusher / Driller  / A/D  / Derrickman / Drill Crew  / Mud
Engineer

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. The  Driller, mud loggers and control room,  will be informed  prior  to
     pumping out or dumping any pits.
    3. Confirm  it is permissible to dump residual mud or fluids overboard,  if not
       transfer any residual mud or fluids to another pit or to the sludge tank.
    4.  Prior to cleaning the tank fill it with seawater to the highest available point
     and circulate through the mud gun line (if fitted) to break up any remaining
       solids. Then pump it out to the sludge tank or overboard.
    5. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    6. Raise a tank entry PTW (Permit to Work),  electrical and mechanical
       isolations.
    7. Before the PTW is signed, the Responsible Person will check the work
       place.
    8. A calibrated and tested gas detector must be used.
    9. Explosion proof drop lights as required, check the electrical cable and plug
      are in good condition.
   10. Check the hoses and connections are in good condition on the high
      pressure wash down gun.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               271


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 272 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   11. Use the correct PPE (goggles or face shield, slicker suits and rubber
       gloves, wellington boots).
   12. Check if the use of the mud pumps will restrict access to the tank.
   13. Complete a tank entry checklist, the gas levels and oxygen levels will be
      recorded.
   14. All Rescue equipment should be on site as per the tank entry checklist.
      Consideration will be given prior to the operation commencing as to how
     an unconscious person would be recovered from the tank and suitable
      equipment should be available as per the plan.
   15. Ensure an Entry Tag is displayed at the entrance of the tank with all the
       relevant information.
   16. Ensure the tank has been adequately ventilated prior to entry. Leave the
       local ventilation running.
   17. Check what is in the pits either side of the tank being cleaned.
   18. Ensure hoses do not block or obstruct doorways, stairs and walkways.
   19. Ensure  all permits have been made out for the task. These must be
      checked at each shift change.
   20. Prior to entering a tank there must a person on standby at the entrance to
      the tank in case of emergency. They are not to enter the tank but to raise
      the alarm in an emergency.

Operation:

    1. Check the pit with the gas detector to ensure it is safe to enter. Record the
       result of the test on the permit to work.
    2. Ensure valves are mechanically isolated prior to entering the tank.
    3.  If unable to dump the dead volume use a Wilden pump (or similar) to
       transfer the residual volume to another pit.
    4.  Ventilate the tank either naturally or mechanically.
    5. Perform a gas check immediately prior to entering the tank.
    6. Inform the control room and immediate supervisor prior to entering the
       tank.
    7. Enter the tank with the gas detector take readings in each corner as high
      as possible. The gas detector will remain in the tank during while the tank
        is cleaned.
    8. Clean out any remaining solids with a water hose, shovel and bucket as
       required.
    9. Once the solids have been removed, use the high pressure wash down
     gun to completely clean the pit including the sides, top and all the beams.
    A tank cleaning system may also be used to clean the pits.
   10. Goggles or a full face visor will be worn when using the high pressure
     wash down gun.
   11. Hose out the bottom of the pit and squeegee any remaining water until the
        pit is completely clean. Use a Wilden pump (or similar), to suck out the
      residue from the sump.
   12. Secure any hatches removed for the operation.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               272


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 273 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

   13. If the work is interrupted for any reason, the tank should be flushed out
     and ventilated prior to entry and the above procedure regarding isolations,
      tank entry checks and gas checks will be done.
   14. Remove all tools from the tank.
   15. Remove all mechanical and electrical isolations.
   16. Sign off the PTW.
   17. Fill the tank with sea water and check for any leaking valves. Empty the
       tank.
   18. Run the agitators once they have been de-isolated.
   19. Inform the control room and immediate supervisor that all work in pit is
      complete.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clear all tools & equipment from work site.
    2. Check out any equipment used, lights, cables, hoses and the rescue
      equipment for any damage.
    3. Review the procedure and JSA.

4.2.10 Miscellaneous


 4.2.10.1    Well Control – Stripping

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure smooth and safe operation while
stripping 5” Drill pipe back to bottom through a closed Annular Preventer during
well control operations.

Scope:

If a well control situation occurs with the Bit off bottom, the need arises to strip
pipe through a closed Annular Preventer without allowing a further influx to occur
and to get the Bit back to bottom to allow the most favourable option to kill the
well.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Night pusher Driller / A/D / Drill Crew

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. The annular preventer must be closed with enough pressure to allow
       stripping to take place but not to leak.
    3. Check the line up of the choke line to the trip tank.
    4. Check all valves and lines for leaks.
    5. Establish good communications between the choke manifold and DCR
        (drillers control room).
    6.  All equipment and hand tools to be in good working order.
    7. Record  all  pressures and volumes and  evaluate  continuously once
       stripping commences.




Operation:



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               274


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 275 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

After a kick has been detected, safely closed in, the pressures stabilised and
recorded, the options will be considered, one of which could be to strip back to
bottom. Extra thought will need to be given if drill pipe centralisers are in use.

    1. Adjust the Annular closing pressure to approx. 550/600 psi; check the trip
      tank volume is static.
    2. Remove any pipe wiper rubbers from below the rotary and close the CMC
      (crown mounted compensator).
    3. Record the  initial shut in casing pressure,  trip tank and stripping tank
       levels. If there is no stripping tank then the trip tank must be used to take
       returns from the choke manifold.
    4. Line up to take returns from the choke manifold to the stripping/trip tank.
       Establish good communications between the choke control unit and the
         driller.
    5. Run in and set the drill pipe in the slips. Close the stab-in valve and bleed
        off the standpipe pressure. Check the volume bled off to determine the
      stab in valve is not leaking.
    6. Break off the top drive and make up the NRV (Non Return Valve) on top of
      the stab-in valve ensuring the plunger has been released. Open the stab-in
      valve to verify the non return valve is holding pressure.
    7. A drop in dart may be used in place of a non return valve as per the
      Operator Well Control Procedure. This would be displaced to the dart sub
       with a mud pump after the top drive is made up to the string again.
    8. Do not make up the next stand before the stab in valve has been opened
       or a leak may go undetected.
    9. Commence stripping in at a constant speed, file down the tong marks on
      the tooljoint as required and apply a coating of pipe dope as the tool joints
      pass the Rotary.
   10. Allow casing pressure to rise by a safety margin depending on M.A.A.S.P.
       (e.g. 50 – 200 psi).
   11. When casing pressure has risen by the safety margin, hold the casing
      pressure constant by manipulating the choke as a stand is stripped in the
       hole.
   12. During this operation the stripping/trip tank volume should increase by the
      volume equivalent to the closed end displacement of the pipe in use e.g.
      2.2 bbl/stand for 5”  drill pipe. This figure is to be agreed by OIM, Rig
      Superintendent, Toolpusher, and Operator Drilling Supervisor.
   13. When the stand is down, it may be filled using the casing fill line from the
       active pit (ensure the mud weight is the same as in the hole).
   14. Continue stripping in stands as in the previous steps, noting volumes
      returned to the stripping/trip tank and volumes used to fill pipe from active
         pit. (Any discrepancies need to be reported and dealt with).
   15. If gas is detected in the stripping/trip tank then  it will be necessary to
       circulate the annulus clean before stripping further.
   16. If it has been proved that the Annular is not leaking and the volume of fluid
      returned from the well is greater than the volume of steel tripped in, the

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               275

      casing pressure should be increased accordingly in order for B.H.P. to
      remain constant.

Post Task Actions:

    1. When the bit is back on bottom or below the influx, the well can be killed
       conventionally using the agreed well control method.
    2. The cause of the influx and its depth in the hole will decide the well control
     method e.g. A swabbed influx can be circulated out using the  driller’s
     method as no mud weight increase will be required.

 4.2.10.2    Fishing

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to ensure a smooth and safe operation while
down hole fishing operations are carried out.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the activities that are to be carried out prior to
and during the down hole fishing operations

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent/Senior Toolpusher / Toolpusher / Driller / A/D

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Preparation:

    1. Hold a Toolbox meeting. Review the procedure and JSA (Job Safety
       Analysis).
    2. Apply the greatest accuracy to all measurements.
    3. Draw a complete sketch of the equipment to be run, specifying lengths,
       inside and outside diameters and a description of each tool.
    4. Ensure that all personnel directly involved in operation are fully acquainted
     and familiar with equipment to be used and its limitations.
    5. The  fishing equipment on the  rig should be  fully inspected. Further
       inspection and maintenance shall be carried out on the rig if in prolonged
      use.
    6. Make a note of any existing marks/signs on the fishing tool for future
       interpretation. Take photographs for reference.
    7. Keep mud properties in good conditions at all times.
    8. Closely monitor the well while tripping and fishing.
    9. Keep the fishing equipment in good working condition and ready for use at
         all times.

Equipment:

The following tools shall be always available on the rig for the various hole sizes
drilled:

    1. Fishing jars to match the drill collars in use.
    2. Bumper subs to match the drill collars in use.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               277


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 278 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    3. Overshot and oversize guides with grapples, baskets and extension subs
       to catch all diameters of tools in hole.
    4. Taper taps for drill pipe body and tool joints.
    5. Junk baskets or Globe-type baskets.
    6. Reverse circulation junk baskets.
    7. Junk subs.
    8. Fishing magnets.
    9.  Milling tools.
   10. Re-dressing tools for 5" and 31/2" sheared DP.
   11. Impression blocks.
   12. Safety joints.

Making up Assembly:

The standard fishing assembly typically consists of the following:

    1. Fishing tool + Jar and Bumper Sub + Drill Collars + Heavy Weight Drill
      Pipe + Drill Pipe.
    2. Use as many drill collars as are in the fish. If the required number of drill
       collars are not available on the rig, a jar accelerator should be used.
    3. A Safety Joint should not be run. Since the Safety Joint will not transmit left
     hand torque,  it would not be possible to back-off below  it using a string
       shot.
    4. However, a Safety Joint could be run between the catching tool and the jar
     when a non releasing tool such as taper tap is being employed.
    5. Avoid any restrictions in the bore of tools, run above the catching tool,
      which would prevent the use of a cutting tool or the back-off shot within the
        fish.
    6. Where losses are expected the use of a Circulation Sub in the fishing
      assembly should be considered.

Fishing Operation:


    1. Be sure that the overshot OD is compatible with the hole diameter and the
      grapple with the fishing neck.
    2. Whenever possible install a lock ring.
    3. When fishing on a tool joint, use a basket grapple with long catch stop.
    4. The catching action of the overshot will cause a crushing force on the fish
      neck.
    5. A regular, smooth shape of the fish neck is necessary for a successful
       operation.
    6. Jarring is only possible only using type SFS, FS and XFS overshots.
    7.  If the fish diameter is near the maximum catch or size, a spiral grapple is
     recommended. On the other hand,  if the fish diameter is considerably
      below the maximum catch size, a basket grapple is preferable.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               278


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 279 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    8.  If the hole is enlarged, use an oversize guide or run a bent drill pipe just
      above the overshot.
    9. When the fish has been milled over, if possible, run an overshot extension
       to avoid catching the fish by the milled shoulder.
   10. In case of re-run check the overshot shape.
   11. Run the fishing assembly, space out as required, and make up the Top
       drive.
   12. RIH to place the overshot to just above the fish and circulate for a few
      minutes to clean the top of the fish. Do not circulate excessively as this
     may enlarge the hole.
   13. Prior to engaging the fish, record the weight of fishing string (up, down and
       rotating) with and without circulation.
   14. To engage a fish, the fishing string is lowered and rotated to the right very
       slowly,  pumping  at minimum  rate.  During  the  engaging  operation,
      continuously monitor the weight indicator and stand pipe pressure.
   15. As the fish is engaged, allow the right hand torque to unwind and pick up
     on the fish, checking the string weight matches the expected weight.
   16. If possible, consideration should be given to circulating bottoms up through
      the fish before pulling out of hole, particularly  if potential reservoirs are
      exposed or penetration rates were high.
   17. When tripping out of the hole with the fish, the string must not be rotated
     and the slips should be set carefully.
   18. If pulling out of the hole wet, flow checks shall be carried out frequently.

Releasing Spear:

    1. Choose the catching size according to casing nominal weight.
    2. The fish will be stressed outwards due to the catching action of the tool.
    3. A regular, smooth shape of the fish is essential for a successful operation.
    4. To allow unlatching of the spear,  if  it is not possible to run an adequate
     number of drill collars above the releasing spear, the use of a bumper sub
        is recommended.
    5.  Install a pack-off on the tool, if circulation is required after latching the fish.
    6. Use the fishing jar if jarring is required. In this case the use of a Spear Stop
        is required. Check the Spear Stop OD when  it is used in open hole and
      use the stop only if hole condition permits.
    7. Perform the fishing job as per overshot procedure.

Taper Tap:

    1. The size of the taper tool should be selected in order to engage the fish
       with the middle of the tapered point.
    2. The taper taps do not allow free passage to the back-off tool.
    3. Excessive torque can damage the tapered thread and swell the top of the
        fish.


                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               279


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 280 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    4.  It is impossible to release the tool once engaged. For this reason its use
      should be considered the last resort and only utilised after consultation
       with Shore Base (Drilling Manager/Superintendent).
    5. Run the fishing assembly, complete with safety joint, space out as required
     and make up the Top Drive.
    6. RIH the taper Tap to just above the fish and circulate a few minutes to
      clean the top of the fish. Do not circulate excessively as this may enlarge
      the hole.
    7. To engage the fish, apply right hand torque. A gradual increase of back
      torque is an indication of successful operation.
    8. Do not rotate the string and set the slips gently while POOH.

Junk Basket:

    1. This procedure is more successful in soft formations.
    2. A reverse circulation type junk basket is preferred to a forward circulation
       type. Use the following parameters:
          • WOB = 2 to 4t
          •  Rotary = 45rpms
          •  Low Pump Rate (1/2 pump rate while drilling).
    3. Core approximately 20cm. Pick up to allow the junk on the side of the
      basket to fall into the pilot hole, then proceed coring a further +/-50cm.
    4.  Pull the junk basket out of the hole.
    5.  After use, careful inspection and refurbishment is necessary.

Fishing Magnet:

    1. Magnets can be successfully used but only in hard formations to retrieve
      small steel objects such as bit cones, bearings, slips, tong pins and milling
       cuttings.
    2. Avoid putting weight on the fish as it may embed it in the hole.
    3. Fishing magnets may be run on wireline or on pipe.
    4. Wireline operations have the advantage of speed and economy.
    5. Pipe operation has the great advantage of utilising the circulation holes in
      the magnet to remove sediment from above the fish.

Jarring:

    1.  Jarring should be done with the Top Drive. If the use of a Top Drive is not
       possible, secure the elevator latch by using a piece of rope or chain.
    2.  Prior to jarring check the drill line load sensor. Ensure the weight indicator
      readings are accurate and that the dead line anchor is secure and free of
       debris. Check the derrick and all equipment for any loose items.
    3. When jarring, the drill floor must be cleared of all non-essential personnel.



                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               280


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 281 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    4.  Prior to jarring, mark the drill string at the rotary table at neutral weight and
      note the state of the tide.
    5. Check the drill line usage, slip and cut if necessary. When sustained jarring
        is carried out, the drill line should be slipped at regular intervals, depending
     on the particular situation. Also check the derrick, lifting equipment and
       travelling block attachment bolts.
    6. Always allow the jars to trip within their safe working load. Wait until the
       jars have tripped before pulling the string further. Never exceed the safe
      working limit without confirmation that the jars have tripped.
    7.  After jarring, check the Top Drive as per the maintenance and operating
       specification.

Milling:

Junk Mills:

Used to mill  all type of junk, including rock  bit cones, reamer cutters, items
dropped through the rotary, drill pipe cemented inside and outside, etc.

Pilot Mills:

Designed to mill drill pipe, casing, tubing, wash pipe, safety joint, swaged casing,
etc.

Taper Mills:

Generally used to eliminate restrictions or to mill through collapsed casing

Washover Shoes:

Designed to mill away formation or tool obstructions such as stabilizer blades,
reamer cutters, expanded packers and bit bodies which may be holding the drill
or tubing string in the hole

Special Mills (Window mills, Watermelon mills, etc.):

For casing side-tracking systems

The following are general guidelines for the use of milling tools:

    1.  Milled cuttings are much heavier than  drilling cuttings. Therefore, mud
       viscosity should be increased or high viscosity pills should be pumped to
      help in carrying the steel cuttings out of the hole.
    2.  Oil based mud has poor carrying capabilities and should be avoided
      whenever possible. Polymer muds are most suitable for milling.
    3. Never mill faster than it is possible to remove the cuttings.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               281


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 282 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by

    4. Magnets placed in the flow line will help in removing metal particles from
        drilling mud. Removal of mill cuttings and debris reduces the wear on mud
     pumps and other equipment.
    5. A junk sub placed in the string above the mill can aid in catching the larger
       cuttings.
    6. Whenever possible, a stabiliser should be run within 20-30m above the mill
       to prevent it from moving eccentrically.
    7. The stabiliser OD should not exceed the dressed OD of the mill.
    8. Always start rotating, with low rpm about 1m above the fish. Lower onto
      the fish and adjust the weight and the rotary speed to obtain satisfactory
       penetration.
    9. Generally the most efficient milling rates are obtained by running the rotary
       at 80 to 100rpm. Milling with washover shoes is an exception and are
       usually more efficient at speeds of 60 to 80rpm. Continuously monitor the
      torque indicator during milling operations.
   10. Reading the cuttings’ is essential to evaluate the performance of the mill.
     The ideal cuttings are usually 1-2mm thick and 25-30mm long. If cuttings
      are thin long stringers, penetration rates are probably too low and weight
     on the  mill should be increased.  If fish-scale type cuttings are being
       returned,  penetration  rate   will  improve  by  decreasing  weight  and
       increasing rpm.
   11. The type and stability of the fish (cemented or not) together with the
      hardness of the fish and/or cement are factors that affect milling rates.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Clean up the drill floor.
    2. Clean and refurbish any fishing gear used.
    3. Order replacement parts for any fishing gear used.
    4. RIH to circulate and condition the mud.
    5. Closely monitor the well.





                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               282

 4.2.10.3   Emergency Hang off

Purpose:

The purpose of this procedure is to control the operation of hanging off the drill
string in the BOP.

Scope:

The following procedure describes the required activities which need to be carried
on the rig floor, prior to and during hanging off the drill string in the BOP.

Responsible Persons:

Rig Superintendent / Toolpusher / Driller / Sub Sea eng / DP

Procedure:

Safety Precautions and Precautions:

    1. Inform the Rig superintendent and toolpusher upon receiving the yellow
      alarm notification.
    2.  All personnel involved must be trained to be familiar with the hang off
      sequences in various situations as describe here below.
    3. Space-out calculations must be posted in the DCR.
    4.  It should be noted that  if the length of the stands of  drill pipe vary
      considerably care should be taken when closing the hang off rams.
.
Upon notification of a Yellow Alarm the following procedures will be carried
out.

Drilling operations:

    1.  Pull the bit off bottom and stop rotating.
    2. Space out the tool joint as per hang off calculation sheet.
    3. Stop pumping.
    4. Note the string weight.
    5. Close the hang off rams, hang off the string and close the ram wedge
       locks.
    6. Adjust the CMC to support only the string weight from the surface to the
     BOP.
    7. Wait  for  further  notification from the DP operators and management
       instructions.

                   Iran Amir Kabir Rig Specific Drilling Procedures               283


────────────────────────────────────────────
[ صفحه 284 از 286 ]
────────────────────────────────────────────
                         Issue Date
                                                          Unit:                     Iran Amir Kabir
                          January 2011
                         Revision No.
                                            Manual Nr:
                               0
                         Revision Date                                             Drilling Operations
                                          Document Title:
                                                                     Procedures Manual
                       Prepared by                        Reviewed and
                        K.Tiersma                          Approved by


Tripping operations with drill pipe in the BOP:

    1. Stop tripping.
    2. Space out the tool joint as per hang off calculation sheet.
    3. Open the CMC.
    4. Note the string weight.
    5. Close the hang off rams, hang off the string and close the ram wedge
       locks.
    6. Adjust the CMC to support only the string weight from the surface to the
     BOP.
    7. Wait  for  further  notification from the DP operators and management
       instructions.

Running casing w/ last stand of the landing string:

    1. Don’t run the cementing head assembly.
    2. Make up one more stand of drill pipe and open the CMC.
    3. Land casing hanger on the well head and adjust CMC to support only
      running string weight. The neutral point should be at the running tool level
       to ease for releasing.
    4. Release the running tool and pull the same above the BOP.
    5. Wait  for  further  notification from the DP operators and management
       instructions.
    6. Monitor the well.

Post Task Actions:

    1. Closely monitor the situation and be prepared to shear the pipe and
      disconnect the LMRP on a red alarm.
    2.  If the Yellow alarm is cancelled take the noted string weight.
    3. Open the wedgelocks and open the rams.
    4. Flow check the well and if stable continue the previous operations.
